●
"What is happening here?"

THICK PROMPT TEST
"● there" / "yellow circle there"
"What happens next?"

One reconstructs the operative past.

The other composes the operative future.

That is the inversion.

## Response:
9/9/2026, 8:23:11 PM

> **Searched 8 websites**
>
> 
>
> Worked for 11s

Yes. The right move is to stop treating **thick description** and **thick prompting** as two techniques applied to language from outside. Each is itself a **different language-game**, with different players, valid moves, criteria of success, directions of attention, and consequences.

Wittgenstein's definition is useful because a language-game is not merely a vocabulary or grammar. It is the whole of **language and the activities into which it is woven**. His own list includes commanding, reporting, hypothesizing, joking, translating, asking, thanking, and many other practices. ([Stanford Encyclopedia of Philosophy](https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/wittgenstein/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)) Geertz then gives us a way to ask what kind of act is occurring within such a practice: the same eyelid movement can be twitch, wink, parody, rehearsal, or fake-wink because it belongs to different layered structures of socially intelligible action. ([Stanford HCI Group](https://hci.stanford.edu/courses/cs376/private/readings/geertz_thick_description.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

So I would describe the Yellow Circle practices like this:

| Practice / language-game | Characteristic move | What participants attend to | What counts as success | Difference that makes the difference |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Thick description** | “What was that?” | behavior + social code + history + relation + circumstance | identifying what the behavior counted as | context that changes twitch → wink → parody |
| **Thick prompting** | “Make this happen.” | desired action + scene + shared history + available operations | an appropriate next action becomes possible | context that changes words into the intended operation |
| **Deictic coordination** | “There” + ☞ | joint attention, timing, current scene | interlocutor acts at the intended place | where/when the finger points |
| **Giving an order** | “Put the circle there.” | imperative force, object, destination | addressee performs requested action | recognizing the utterance as a command/request rather than description |
| **Following an order** | receiving “there” and acting | current task + learned practice | right object goes to right place | knowing what counts as compliance |
| **Description/report** | “There is a yellow circle there.” | existing state of world | utterance fits world | whether the circle actually exists |
| **Criticism** | “That's not a circle.” | artifact relative to a criterion | discrepancy becomes articulable | difference between present object and norm |
| **Correction/repair** | “No, rounder.” | prior failed coordination | subsequent action repairs breakdown | which feature of previous result is treated as wrong |
| **Teaching** | “No, this is what I mean by circle.” | examples, contrasts, recurrence | another participant acquires the distinction | which differences are preserved/rejected across examples |
| **Imitation/example** | “Make another like this ●” | invariants of exemplar | new artifact is accepted as another instance | which properties of exemplar count as constitutive |
| **Aspect seeing** | “That's a rabbit.” | same marks under another organization | new continuation becomes intelligible | prompt changes duck → rabbit without changing marks |
| **Programming** | `circle(128,128,48)` | formally admitted distinctions | machine state changes according to rule | syntax/values recognized by interpreter |
| **GUI manipulation** | select yellow + ellipse + drag | location, tool state, visibility, gesture | interface produces expected state | spatial/state context that makes drag count as drawing |
| **Generative prompting** | “Not that. More like this.” | conversation + artifact + model inference | local coordination progressively stabilizes | critique changes machine's next transformation |

The crucial distinction is that **the same sentence can belong to several different games**.

Take:

> **That's not a circle.**

In a geometry lesson, it may be a **classification**.

In an art critique, an **evaluation**.

In an argument, a **challenge**.

In a generative interface, a **repair instruction**.

In a joke, it may be deliberate misclassification.

In a philosophy seminar, it could be a Magritte-like claim about representation.

Geertz would insist that recording the sentence alone is a thin description. To know what happened, you need the hierarchy of structures within which saying it counted as *criticizing*, *correcting*, *joking*, or *teaching*. That is exactly the point of his Ryle example: identical bodily movement can belong to radically different acts because “what someone is doing” includes the socially established code and situation within which the movement occurs. ([Perlego](https://www.perlego.com/book/679694/the-interpretation-of-cultures-text-only-pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

And Wittgenstein gives the complementary point: **the meaning is inseparable from what people are doing with the expression**. `Slab!` is not merely a shortened descriptive sentence whose missing semantic content we need to reconstruct. In the builders' practice, calling “slab” and fetching a slab constitute the language-game. Later versions of Wittgenstein's builder example explicitly introduce numerals, “this,” “there,” and pointing, which makes your Yellow Circle experiment uncannily close to the canonical case. ([Google Sites](https://sites.google.com/view/philosophy-texts/20th-century/analytic-philosophy/ludwig-wittgenstein/philosophical-investigations?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

So your two central games are almost mirror images.

### 1. The Geertz game: **What just happened?**

The ethnographer arrives after or alongside action:

```text
VISIBLE MOVEMENT
      ↓
recover:
code
history
participants
occasion
intentional organization
possible alternative readings
      ↓
WHAT ACT WAS THIS?
```

The relevant distinction is **classification of an accomplished act**.

The difference between blink and wink changes the *description that is adequate to what happened*.

Its characteristic failure is:

```text
WINK
↓
described as:
EYELID CONTRACTION
```

Nothing factually false about the muscles, but you have missed **the act**.

That is thinness.

### 2. The prompting game: **What should happen next?**

Here the participant is not reconstructing an accomplished act.

They are trying to establish enough of a shared game that another participant can continue it:

```text
"There." ☞
      ↓
recover/recruit:
current activity
referent
prior artifact
gesture
possible action
expected consequence
      ↓
WHAT MOVE SHOULD FOLLOW?
```

Its characteristic failure is:

```text
"There." ☞
↓
wrong object moves
```

The system may have parsed every word correctly and still failed to understand the **move**.

So the strong symmetry is not simply past/future:

$$
\text{thick description}:
\quad
\text{behavior} + \text{game}
\rightarrow
\text{act}
$$

$$
\text{thick prompting}:
\quad
\text{utterance} + \text{game}
\rightarrow
\text{next act}
$$

The **language-game is the missing variable in both equations**.

That gives you a better formulation than “context.”

> **Thick description asks which language-game makes this behavior intelligible as this act. Thick prompting asks which language-game must be made available for this utterance to have this consequence.**

And then critique becomes the hinge between them:

```text
● generated

"That's not a circle."
        ↑
THICK DESCRIPTION:
what did the system just make?
how should this result be classified?

        ↓
THICK PROMPTING:
given that discrepancy,
what should the system do next?
```

The same utterance faces backward and forward.

This is where Geertz and Wittgenstein fuse beautifully.

Geertz gives you the **vertical axis**:

```text
movement
↓
gesture
↓
wink
↓
parodied wink
↓
rehearsal of parodied wink
...
```

Increasing layers of **what this is an instance of**.

Wittgenstein gives you the **horizontal axis**:

```text
move₁ → response₂ → move₃ → response₄
```

The unfolding sequence of **what can appropriately happen next within the practice**.

So you can model a language-game with two coordinates:

$$
G=(T,C)
$$

where:

- $T$ = **thickness**, the hierarchy of distinctions necessary to know what the present act counts as;
- $C$ = **continuation**, the range of next moves made appropriate by that interpretation.

That is useful because it lets you define understanding operationally:

> **To understand an act is simultaneously to locate it within the right hierarchy of descriptions and to know what kinds of continuation it licenses.**

Duck versus rabbit demonstrates it. Wink versus blink demonstrates it. Yellow dot versus button demonstrates it. Yellow circle versus Piet instruction demonstrates it.

The strongest test, then, is not merely:

> “Can the system name what it sees?”

It is:

> **After naming it that way, does it know what moves have become possible?**

If:

```text
● = BUTTON
```

then:

```text
"press it"
```

becomes grammatical.

If:

```text
● = SUN
```

then:

```text
"make it set"
```

becomes grammatical.

If:

```text
● = NODE
```

then:

```text
"connect it"
```

becomes grammatical.

If:

```text
● = YELLOW CIRCLE
```

then:

```text
"make it larger"
```

becomes grammatical.

Same visible mark. **Different practice, therefore different grammar of continuation.**

And that gives you a compact definition I think is stronger than the earlier one:

> **A language-game is a socially or operationally stabilized way of deciding both what the present difference counts as and what differences may appropriately follow from it.**

Geertz thickens the **present act**. Wittgenstein situates it among its **possible continuations**. Thick prompting turns that whole structure forward, using enough of the game to make the next move possible.

---

**Sources:**

- [Ludwig Wittgenstein (Stanford Encyclopedia of Philosophy)](https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/wittgenstein/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- [The Interpretation of Cultures: Selected Essays](https://hci.stanford.edu/courses/cs376/private/readings/geertz_thick_description.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- [[PDF] The Interpretation of Cultures (Text Only) by Clifford Geertz | 9780006862604, 9780008219475](https://www.perlego.com/book/679694/the-interpretation-of-cultures-text-only-pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- [Philosophy Texts - Philosophical Investigations](https://sites.google.com/view/philosophy-texts/20th-century/analytic-philosophy/ludwig-wittgenstein/philosophical-investigations?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

Or prompt superstition helps me think well fo myself as someone who knows how to command control machine which may make me better at it

## Response:
9/9/2026, 8:25:02 PM

Yes. That changes the account substantially. **Prompt superstition can be instrumentally useful even when its theory of the machine is false.**

The crucial split is:

$$
\text{Does the ritual improve the model?}
$$

versus

$$
\text{Does the ritual improve the person operating the model?}
$$

Those are different causal questions.

Suppose you believe:

> “I should first tell the model exactly what role it has, state the constraints, define success, then tell it to critique itself.”

Maybe some pieces genuinely alter model behavior. Maybe half are redundant. Maybe one phrase is pure superstition.

But performing that ritual can still make **you** do something valuable:

```text
vague intention
↓
pause
↓
name desired outcome
↓
notice assumptions
↓
state constraints
↓
anticipate failure
↓
evaluate response
```

So even if:

```text
MAGIC PHRASE
↛
BETTER MODEL
```

it may be true that:

```text
PROMPT RITUAL
→
BETTER ATTENTION
→
BETTER OPERATOR
→
BETTER INTERACTION
```

That is much more interesting than simply debunking prompt superstition.

The superstition can function as a **cognitive jig**.

Just like a carpenter's jig constrains movement so that a difficult cut becomes repeatable, a prompt ritual can constrain your own thinking:

> Did I actually say what I wanted?

> What counts as success?

> What am I leaving implicit?

> What should remain invariant?

> What would failure look like?

You may initially believe you are **commanding the machine correctly**. But what the ritual is partly doing is **teaching you to formulate operational descriptions**.

So prompt engineering may have had a pedagogical effect even where its folk theory of models was terrible.

> **The ritual trains the operator under the guise of controlling the machine.**

That is a strong idea.

It also connects directly to language-games. A novice has no stable practice for talking to the machine. They encounter an opaque system whose behavior varies. So they acquire formulas:

```text
"You are an expert..."
"Think step by step..."
"Ask me questions first..."
"Do not..."
"Output exactly..."
```

These may function like the exaggerated rule-following of someone learning a foreign language.

A novice speaker thinks:

> *This is the rule.*

A fluent speaker eventually knows:

> *This is one move that works under these circumstances.*

So there could be a developmental sequence:

```text
UNCERTAINTY
↓
SUPERSTITION
↓
RITUAL
↓
REPEATABLE PRACTICE
↓
DISCRIMINATION
↓
EXPERTISE
↓
RULES CAN BE DROPPED
```

That is extremely close to what we were just saying about thick prompts becoming thin as a language-game stabilizes.

Early:

```text
"Act as an expert software engineer.
First inspect the repository.
Do not modify unrelated files.
Run the tests.
Check your work..."
```

Later:

```text
"Fix this."
```

The apparent disappearance of rules may mean the operator and system have acquired enough shared competence that the ritual scaffolding is no longer required.

So **prompt superstition may be an intermediate stage in the acquisition of a language-game**.

That prevents us from dividing practices too quickly into:

```text
TRUE TECHNIQUE
vs
FALSE SUPERSTITION
```

A better distinction is:

```text
MACHINE-OPERATIVE
changes model behavior reliably

OPERATOR-OPERATIVE
changes human attention/behavior reliably

COORDINATION-OPERATIVE
stabilizes interaction between them

RITUAL-RESIDUAL
persists after none of those effects remain
```

One prompt phrase could even move through all four categories historically.

At first:

```text
"Be concise."
```

may materially constrain a weak model.

Later the model no longer needs it, but the phrase reminds the operator what they want.

Later still both parties already share that expectation.

Yet the phrase remains because:

> that's how I prompt.

Now it has become ritual residue.

But even ritual residue isn't necessarily useless. It can help establish a **stance**.

And I think “stance” is important here.

When you sit down and write a highly structured prompt, you may be constructing yourself as:

```text
<someone capable of specifying>
<someone directing a process>
<someone who can diagnose failure>
<someone with authority to reject output>
```

That self-positioning can affect the subsequent interaction.

You become less likely to accept the first answer.

You notice discrepancies.

You issue corrections.

You develop vocabulary for transformations.

You learn which distinctions affect results.

So the prompt can be **performative toward the prompter**.

> **It does not merely tell the machine who it is. It tells the human who they are in relation to the machine.**

That is where the "You are an expert..." prompt becomes funny.

Surface reading:

```text
USER → MODEL
"You are an expert."
```

But pragmatically it can also enact:

```text
USER → SELF
"I am now conducting an expert-style interaction."
```

Or:

```text
USER → RELATIONSHIP
"We are entering the language-game in which
technical precision, critique, and revision count."
```

That is metapragmatics again.

The phrase may be less like a machine instruction and more like saying:

> **“This is play.”**

Bateson's frame.

It tells everyone, including the speaker, **what kind of interaction comes next**.

Then prompt superstition becomes analogous to ritual more broadly. A ritual does not need to achieve the causal mechanism participants attribute to it in order to organize attention, expectation, sequence, role, commitment, and coordination.

That suggests a much better term than merely “superstition” for some cases:

> **operative ritual**

An operative ritual may contain a false causal theory yet still produce real effects by reorganizing the participants.

And now there is a beautiful Ashby connection.

The ritual may increase the user's **regulatory variety**.

Before learning prompting rituals, the user's repertoire is:

```text
"make it better"
"no"
"try again"
```

Afterward:

```text
preserve X
vary Y
compare A/B
give three alternatives
explain failure
use this exemplar
retain structure
change only tone
```

The person now possesses more distinct interventions for responding to different failure states.

So even a partly superstitious prompting culture can train:

> **requisite variety in the operator.**

That may genuinely make someone better at commanding or coordinating with machines.

The problem begins when the ritual prevents further discrimination.

Expertise should eventually let you discover:

```text
this part is essential
this part merely helps me think
this part establishes a useful frame
this part used to work
this part does nothing
this part actively hurts
```

So perhaps:

> **Prompt superstition is productive while it increases the operator's capacity to notice and control differences. It becomes pathological when the ritual itself becomes immune to differences.**

That is Bateson again.

Good superstition:

```text
ritual
→ practice
→ experimentation
→ finer discrimination
```

Bad superstition:

```text
ritual
→ success
→ sacred rule
→ counterevidence ignored
```

And that gives you a very clean thesis:

> **Prompt engineering may be partly a folk pedagogy of machine control: users invent rituals to manage an opaque system, and through those rituals they train themselves to notice goals, constraints, state, failure, and correction. The folk theory may be wrong while the apprenticeship is real.**

Or shorter:

> **The magic words may not train the machine. They may train the magician.**

I’d refine it around **one central wager rather than one node per thinker**: *a language-game determines which differences are allowed to become operative, and prompting changes where those requisite differences are carried*. The Yellow Circle then becomes the experimental hub, not merely another example.

I would compress the chat into **one spine, four secondary branches, and three experimental instruments**. The graph was starting to become “thinker → thinker → thinker.” The stronger structure is **problem → mechanism → operational consequence → experiment**.

### Central wager

> **A language-game is a regime for deciding which differences are allowed to make a difference. Thickness names how many task-relevant distinctions the coupled situation makes recoverable. Programming, HCI, prompting, and worldtexting differ chiefly in where those distinctions are carried and who must attend to them.**

The central chain is:

```text
DIFFERENCE
    ↓
DISTINCTION
    ↓
COORDINATION
    ↓
LANGUAGE-GAME
    ↓
OPERATIVE DESCRIPTION
    ↓
ACTION / WORLD TRANSFORMATION
    ↓
FEEDBACK
    ↓
NEW DISTINCTIONS
    ↺
```

And the **Yellow Circle** is the instrument through which every link can be perturbed.

### The zettels I would treat as the actual spine

| ID | Claim worth preserving |
|---|---|
| **ZET-DIFFERENCE-MAKES-DIFFERENCE** | A language is not merely a representation system. It determines which differences may become consequential. |
| **ZET-LANGUAGING** | Communication does not begin with shared rules. Recurrent coordination stabilizes distinctions that can become tokens for further coordination. |
| **ZET-LANGUAGE-GAME** | Understanding something as X includes knowing what continuations become appropriate when it counts as X. |
| **ZET-THICK-DESCRIPTION** | Reconstruct the contextual differentiation necessary for a behavior to count as a particular act. |
| **ZET-THICK-PROMPTING** | Construct or recruit the contextual differentiation necessary for an utterance to count as a particular operative move. |
| **ZET-LAW-REQUISITE-DESCRIPTION** | A coupled descriptive system must preserve every distinction whose loss could require a different action. |
| **ZET-ATTENTION-TAX** | Attention tax is the local cost paid by a participant for supplying some portion of requisite description. |
| **ZET-DESCRIPTION-MIGRATION** | Description does not disappear when prompts become shorter. It migrates into conventions, models, artifacts, interfaces, histories, defaults, and feedback. |
| **ZET-WORLDTEXTING** | Operative description changes the situation it describes and thereby changes the conditions of subsequent description. |
| **ZET-WORLD-TO-WORLD** | The deeper computational object is often not text → text but current world + desired difference → next world. |
| **ZET-CRITICISM-AS-PROGRAMMING** | In generative systems, “that's not a circle” can begin as judgment and immediately acquire world-changing direction of fit. |
| **ZET-GUI-SPATIAL-LANGUAGE** | A GUI spatializes grammar: lexicon becomes visible controls, syntax becomes location/sequence, deixis becomes pointer/selection, pragmatics becomes state. |
| **ZET-TASK-OF-TRANSLATOR** | Software repeatedly translates lived concerns into representations tractable to another practice; the stronger goal is to let the receiving technical language be changed by the source mode of intending. |
| **ZET-IMPLIED-USER** | Every interface encodes a wager about what its reader/operator already knows, notices, remembers, and can do. |
| **ZET-EIGENFORM** | What appears to be a stable object may instead be the invariant produced by recurrent operations of distinction. |
| **ZET-ASPECT-INSTABILITY** | Hold pixels fixed and perturb context. Measure how little linguistic change is required to produce a large change in what the artifact is and what actions it licenses. |
| **ZET-PROMPT-SUPERSTITION** | Prompt rituals can survive weak causal theories because they may train the operator, stabilize a stance, or organize attention even when the supposed magic words are unnecessary. |
| **ZET-REQUISITE-NONALIGNMENT** | Successful coordination needs enough alignment to act together and enough surviving difference to permit correction. |
| **ZET-YELLOW-CIRCLE** | Hold the artifact constant and vary the language-game to expose where operational distinctions reside. |
| **ZET-CHINDOGU-YELLOW-CIRCLE** | Maximize requisite machinery while holding desired perceptual difference constant. |
| **ZET-PROMPT-GOLF** | Minimize human-explicit description while measuring how much operative description has been displaced elsewhere. |

### The three experiments should organize the graph

**Yellow Circle = invariance test.**

```text
same apparent artifact
+
different language
→
what had to become relevant?
```

SVG makes `cx`, `cy`, `r`, and `fill` consequential. Logo makes heading, turning, and sequence consequential. Put-That-There makes gesture, timing, shared scene, and prior coordination consequential. The result stays approximately the same while the **regime of relevant differences changes**.

**Duck-Rabbit = instability test.**

```text
same artifact
+
tiny contextual difference
→
different object
→
different valid continuations
```

`duck` licenses beak, swimming, quacking. `rabbit` licenses ears, hopping, burrow. More computationally: the same yellow dot can become `SUN`, `BUTTON`, `NODE`, `TARGET`, or `PROGRAM`, and therefore make `set`, `press`, `connect`, `hit`, or `execute` grammatical. It measures how strongly a prompt can **retype a world without redrawing it**.

**Chindōgu Prompt Golf = displacement test.**

```text
minimum utterance
+
maximum operative apparatus
→
minimum world difference
```

Eventually:

```text
☞
→ 14 representations
→ 8 programs
→ 4 agents
→ GUI
→ renderer
→ verifier
→ repair
→ ●
```

This tests the law of requisite description because shortening the visible prompt cannot simply destroy required distinctions. You follow each one and ask **where it migrated**.

The graph then has a very clear conceptual center:

```text
                     WHAT DIFFERENCES COUNT?
                              │
                 ┌────────────┴────────────┐
                 │                         │
          WHAT IS THIS ACT?         WHAT SHOULD HAPPEN?
                 │                         │
        THICK DESCRIPTION           THICK PROMPTING
                 │                         │
                 └────────────┬────────────┘
                              │
                    REQUISITE DESCRIPTION
                              │
                    WHERE IS IT CARRIED?
                              │
          ┌───────────┬───────┼────────┬───────────┐
        prompt      model   artifact  world      history
          └───────────┴───────┼────────┴───────────┘
                              │
                        ATTENTION TAX
                              │
                    OPERATIVE DIFFERENCE
                              │
                       WORLD₀ → WORLD₁
                              │
                           feedback
                              ↺
```

And I would make **one terminological change** to the current field. Do not define thick prompting primarily as “the reverse of thick description.” That is an excellent discovery mechanism, but it makes the concept dependent on Geertz.

The independent definition is stronger:

> **Prompt thickness is the degree to which the distinctions required for appropriate continuation are recoverable from the coupled situation.**

Then the inversion becomes a result:

> **Thick description asks which distinctions must be recovered to know what an action was. Thick prompting asks which distinctions must be recoverable for the next action to become appropriate.**

And the largest claim underneath the graph becomes:

> **Programming languages, GUIs, prompts, examples, gestures, codebases, and conversational histories are alternative arrangements for carrying requisite distinctions across a coordination problem.**

That is the graph I would build the rest of the Yellow Circle work around.

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-WITTGENSTEIN-DEIXIS-008

TITLE:
The Builder’s Game Already Contains THERE, THIS, Pointing, and Colour

SOURCE:
Ludwig Wittgenstein - Philosophical Investigations - §8. ([Wiley-Blackwell](https://www.blackwellpublishing.com/content/bpl_images/content_store/sample_chapter/9780631231271/Wittgenstein.pdf))

PASSAGE:
The expanded builder language adds “there” and “this,” used with pointing gestures, plus colour samples and commands such as “d-slab-there.”

RESEARCH OBJECT:
The formal resemblance between Wittgenstein’s expanded builder language and Put-That-There.

LOCAL MOVE:
Upgrades Put-That-There from a loose Wittgensteinian analogy to a striking structural collision: object term + colour + deixis + pointing + commanded placement already coexist in §8.

SOURCE TERMS:
language-game; there; this; pointing gesture; colour samples; order; building site.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
The canonical philosophical example used to explain language-games already looks remarkably like a multimodal graphical interface command.

QUESTION:
Did Bolt or the Architecture Machine tradition encounter Wittgenstein’s builder example directly?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Is multimodal deixis a special HCI innovation, or a computational realization of a much older account of how words acquire operational force through activity?

MECHANISM:
Speech leaves location and object selection incomplete; gesture supplies those distinctions inside a trained practice.

FORMAL SHIFT:
WORD → REFERENT becomes WORD + GESTURE + PRACTICE → APPROPRIATE ACTION.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
[WITTGENSTEIN SOURCE DESCRIPTION] “there” and “this” are used with pointing; the assistant acts according to the order.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
COMMAND = lexical distinctions + deictic binding + learned response practice.

TENSION:
Formal resemblance is not evidence of historical influence.

MISSING:
Bolt’s intellectual bibliography beyond the immediate SIGGRAPH paper; Architecture Machine seminar/readings; any direct Wittgenstein citation.

BOUNDARY:
Wittgenstein’s case is imagined philosophical anthropology, not an implementation proposal.

CITATION TRAIL:
Philosophical Investigations §§2, 6-10 → Bolt 1980 Put-That-There.

TEST:
Search Bolt’s dissertation, Nicholas Negroponte’s Architecture Machine publications, Media Room papers, and cited sources for Wittgenstein, language-games, ostension, deixis, or the builder example.

PLATFORM:
Philosophy of language / HCI genealogy.

LINKS:
ZET-LANGUAGE-GAME; ZET-YELLOW-CIRCLE; ZET-DEIXIS; ZET-PUT-THAT-THERE; ZET-METAPRAGMATICS.
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-BOLT-DEIXIS-REPLACES-NAMING-1980

TITLE:
Pointing Can Remove the Need to Know What the Object Is Called

SOURCE:
Richard A. Bolt - “Put-That-There: Voice and Gesture at the Graphics Interface” - SIGGRAPH 1980. ([MIT Media Lab](https://www.media.mit.edu/speech/papers/1980/bolt_SIGGRAPH80_put-that-there.pdf))

PASSAGE:
Bolt says the user can omit colour and shape words and “need not even know what the thing is, or what it is called.”

RESEARCH OBJECT:
Deixis as replacement for explicit object categorization.

LOCAL MOVE:
Strengthens description migration: pointing does not merely compress coordinates; it can bypass lexical classification of the selected object.

SOURCE TERMS:
that; pointing; intended referent; ostensively defined; graphics world; pronomialization.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
A supposedly underspecified pronoun can require less ontology from the user than an explicit noun phrase.

QUESTION:
What information must the system possess for “that” to outperform a categorical description?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can deictic interfaces reduce the requirement that humans adopt the software system’s ontology?

MECHANISM:
The pointing event restricts the candidate referent set sufficiently that lexical attributes become unnecessary for selection.

FORMAL SHIFT:
DESCRIBE OBJECT → SELECT OBJECT IN SITUATION.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Bolt treats “that” as whatever is pointed out within the alternatives present in the scene.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
REFERENT = resolve(pointing_event, scene_state)
rather than
REFERENT = resolve(type + colour + symbolic name).

TENSION:
The reduction in user description is purchased by increased sensing, scene representation, and reference-resolution machinery.

MISSING:
Exact implementation of referent disambiguation when multiple objects intersect or nearly intersect the pointing ray.

BOUNDARY:
Bolt’s claim concerns a deliberately simple graphical microworld.

CITATION TRAIL:
Bolt 1980 → deictic reference → description migration → requisite description.

TEST:
Construct scenes with progressively more candidate objects and measure when pointing alone ceases to supply enough requisite distinction.

PLATFORM:
Multimodal HCI.

LINKS:
ZET-WITTGENSTEIN-DEIXIS-008; ZET-DESCRIPTION-MIGRATION; ZET-LAW-REQUISITE-DESCRIPTION; ZET-YELLOW-CIRCLE.
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-BOLT-EXEMPLAR-AS-PROGRAM-1980

TITLE:
Put-That-There Already Makes One Artifact a Model for Changing Another

SOURCE:
Richard A. Bolt - “Put-That-There: Voice and Gesture at the Graphics Interface” - SIGGRAPH 1980. ([MIT Media Lab](https://www.media.mit.edu/speech/papers/1980/bolt_SIGGRAPH80_put-that-there.pdf))

PASSAGE:
Bolt proposes “Make that ... like that”; the second pointed item becomes the system’s “model” for changing the first.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
The exemplar as operative description.

LOCAL MOVE:
Pushes artifact-as-prompt backward to 1980: an existing graphical object can supply transformation criteria without those criteria being verbalized.

SOURCE TERMS:
make that; like that; model; copy; pointing; attributes.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
The artifact is no longer merely output. It becomes an instruction source for subsequent transformation.

QUESTION:
Which properties of the exemplar are transferred, and which are ignored?

DEEPER QUESTION:
When an example acts as a program, where is the transformation grammar that determines what “like” preserves?

MECHANISM:
One gesture binds the target; another binds an exemplar; the application maps attributes from exemplar to target.

FORMAL SHIFT:
TEXTUAL SPECIFICATION → EXEMPLAR-BASED SPECIFICATION.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Bolt states that the second referenced item becomes the “model” for change and replaces the first in a copy-like operation.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
TRANSFORM(target, exemplar, transfer_policy).

TENSION:
An exemplar cannot specify its own relevance relation; a transfer policy must still decide which similarities count.

MISSING:
Implementation details defining the attribute set copied in the Media Room system.

BOUNDARY:
This is not a quine and does not show that artifacts intrinsically contain their own instructions.

CITATION TRAIL:
Bolt 1980 → exemplar transformation → Geertz model-for/model-of → generative reference prompting.

TEST:
Use the same yellow-circle exemplar under commands “make that like that,” “same colour,” “same shape,” and “copy that”; compare which invariants must be supplied linguistically.

PLATFORM:
Multimodal graphics / exemplar programming.

LINKS:
ZET-YELLOW-CIRCLE; ZET-MODEL-OF-MODEL-FOR; ZET-QUINE-LANGUAGE-GAME; ZET-OPERATIVE-DESCRIPTION.
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-WITTGENSTEIN-ASPECT-ACTION-074

TITLE:
Aspect Seeing Already Changes How an Order Is Carried Out

SOURCE:
Ludwig Wittgenstein - Philosophical Investigations - §74. ([Wiley-Blackwell](https://www.blackwellpublishing.com/content/bpl_images/content_store/sample_chapter/9780631231271/Wittgenstein.pdf))

PASSAGE:
Wittgenstein says two people seeing the same schematic cube differently may carry out “Bring me something like this” differently.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
Aspect perception as a determinant of operative continuation.

LOCAL MOVE:
Strengthens the Duck-Rabbit branch: aspect is not merely alternate labeling; it can change compliance with an instruction.

SOURCE TERMS:
see; sample; use; rules; schematic drawing; order; something like this.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
The visual stimulus can remain fixed while the correct next action changes.

QUESTION:
Can aspect instability be measured as divergence in action rather than divergence in verbal classification?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Does recognizing an object amount partly to acquiring a grammar of possible continuations?

MECHANISM:
A way of seeing organizes a sample under a rule of use; that organization determines what counts as an analogous object.

FORMAL SHIFT:
SAME IMAGE → DIFFERENT ASPECT → DIFFERENT VALID ACTION.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Wittgenstein directly connects seeing a sample differently with using it differently.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
ASPECT_INSTABILITY(x) = change in licensed action-space under minimal contextual perturbation while x remains invariant.

TENSION:
Wittgenstein does not offer a computational metric or claim every aspect switch is linguistically caused.

MISSING:
Experimental HCI and vision-language work measuring prompt-induced changes in action around invariant visual stimuli.

BOUNDARY:
Do not reduce seeing-as to classification labels alone.

CITATION TRAIL:
Philosophical Investigations §§73-74 → aspect perception → Yellow Dot / Duck-Rabbit experiment.

TEST:
Hold one yellow dot pixel-identical; prime it as BUTTON, SUN, NODE, TARGET, or PROGRAM; then issue the same or analogous follow-up and compare action selection.

PLATFORM:
Philosophy of perception / multimodal agents.

LINKS:
ZET-ASPECT-INSTABILITY; ZET-LANGUAGE-GAME; ZET-YELLOW-CIRCLE; ZET-METAPRAGMATICS.
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-WITTGENSTEIN-RULE-AS-SPELL-234

TITLE:
Correct Rule Following Can Feel Like Being Guided by a Spell

SOURCE:
Ludwig Wittgenstein - Philosophical Investigations - §§232-238. ([Wiley-Blackwell](https://www.blackwellpublishing.com/content/bpl_images/content_store/sample_chapter/9780631231271/Wittgenstein.pdf))

PASSAGE:
Wittgenstein imagines ordinary calculation accompanied by the feeling that rules guide each step “as by a spell.”

RESEARCH OBJECT:
Prompt superstition as phenomenology of competent rule following.

LOCAL MOVE:
Supplies a direct philosophical precursor to the prompt-superstition branch: successful conformity can coexist with a mistaken phenomenology of how the rule causes the action.

SOURCE TERMS:
rule; inspiration; guided; spell; agreement; calculating; training.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
Feeling strongly guided by a symbolic rule supplies no proof that the rule mechanically contains all of its applications.

QUESTION:
When prompt users report that a phrase “makes the model think correctly,” are they identifying an operative distinction or narrating the phenomenology of successful coordination?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can ritualized prompting train real competence while preserving a false causal account of why the competence works?

MECHANISM:
Reliable practice produces agreement; the participant retrospectively experiences the explicit rule as if it generated the successful continuation.

FORMAL SHIFT:
SUCCESSFUL COORDINATION ≠ VERIFIED CAUSAL THEORY OF THE RULE.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Wittgenstein contrasts rule-following, inspiration, training, public regularity, and the feeling of magical guidance.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
PROMPT_RITUAL may be operator-operative even when phrase-level machine causality is unverified.

TENSION:
Wittgenstein is not discussing probabilistic models or superstition in AI use.

MISSING:
Controlled prompt-ablation studies that separate operator effects from model effects.

BOUNDARY:
The analogy concerns rule phenomenology, not a claim that prompting is literally magical thinking.

CITATION TRAIL:
Philosophical Investigations §§232-238 → prompt superstition → operative ritual → prompt ablation.

TEST:
Have users employ a ritual prompt whose model-side components are progressively ablated while preserving the user’s planning ritual; separately measure output and operator performance.

PLATFORM:
Rule-following / human-AI interaction.

LINKS:
ZET-PROMPT-SUPERSTITION; ZET-OPERATIVE-RITUAL; ZET-OPERATIVE-DESCRIPTION-ABLATION.

BIBTEX:
@book{Wittgenstein1953PI, author={Ludwig Wittgenstein}, title={Philosophical Investigations}, year={1953}, publisher={Blackwell}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-WITTGENSTEIN-AGREEMENT-IN-JUDGMENTS-242

TITLE:
A Shared Lexicon Is Insufficient Without Enough Agreement in Judgment

SOURCE:
Ludwig Wittgenstein - Philosophical Investigations - §§240-242. ([Wiley-Blackwell](https://www.blackwellpublishing.com/content/bpl_images/content_store/sample_chapter/9780631231271/Wittgenstein.pdf))

PASSAGE:
Wittgenstein says linguistic communication requires agreement not only in definitions but also in judgments.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
The background stability required for thin prompts to remain actionable.

LOCAL MOVE:
Qualifies the idea that prompt compression can be achieved merely by establishing vocabulary. Stable continuation also depends on recurrently compatible judgments.

SOURCE TERMS:
agreement; language; form of life; judgments; measurement; constancy.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
Two agents can share the definition of “circle” yet still fail to coordinate if they repeatedly disagree over which rendered cases count as circles.

QUESTION:
What minimum judgmental agreement is required for “same,” “better,” “rounder,” or “like this” to function as compressed prompts?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Is the hidden substrate of prompt fluency primarily shared vocabulary, or shared discriminatory practice?

MECHANISM:
Repeated agreement over applications stabilizes what may safely be left unstated in later utterances.

FORMAL SHIFT:
SHARED DEFINITIONS → SHARED CRITERIA OF APPLICATION.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Wittgenstein distinguishes agreement in definitions from agreement in judgments and ties measurement to constancy of results.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
PROMPT_COMPRESSION requires sufficient stability of both lexical mappings and acceptance/rejection judgments.

TENSION:
Agreement in judgment enables communication but does not by itself establish external truth.

MISSING:
A measure distinguishing coordination agreement from world-grounded accuracy in human-model interaction.

BOUNDARY:
Do not turn “form of life” into a computational state variable without qualification.

CITATION TRAIL:
Philosophical Investigations §§240-242 → languaging → requisite nonalignment.

TEST:
Train two interaction histories with identical vocabulary but systematically different acceptance criteria for circle quality; test whether identical thin follow-ups diverge.

PLATFORM:
Language-game theory / evaluation.

LINKS:
ZET-LANGUAGE-GAME; ZET-REQUISITE-NONALIGNMENT; ZET-PROMPT-THICKNESS; ZET-YELLOW-CIRCLE.

BIBTEX:
@book{Wittgenstein1953PI, author={Ludwig Wittgenstein}, title={Philosophical Investigations}, year={1953}, publisher={Blackwell}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-MATURANA-LANGUAGE-NOT-PROMPT-TEXT-2002

TITLE:
For Maturana, Language Is the Flow of Coordination, Not the Isolated Utterance

SOURCE:
Humberto Maturana Romesín - “Autopoiesis, Structural Coupling and Cognition” - 2002. ([Reflexus](https://reflexus.org/wp-content/uploads/Autopoiesis-structural-coupling-and-cognition.pdf))

PASSAGE:
Maturana describes language as living together in recurrent “coordinations of coordinations” of consensual doings.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
The ontological status of a prompt within languaging.

LOCAL MOVE:
Pressures any claim that a prompt string itself constitutes a language. Under Maturana, it becomes linguistic through its place in an ongoing relational flow.

SOURCE TERMS:
languaging; coordinations of coordinations; consensual behaviors; recursive interactions; living together.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
The “prompt” may be analytically the wrong unit if its operative force resides across prompt, response, correction, history, and shared situation.

QUESTION:
What is the minimal interactional unit for studying prompting as languaging?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can an LLM interaction satisfy enough of Maturana’s relational structure to make “languaging” more than a metaphor?

MECHANISM:
Current action perturbs a recurrent coordination whose continuation retrospectively stabilizes the role of the current utterance.

FORMAL SHIFT:
PROMPT → OUTPUT becomes TURN ↔ RESPONSE ↔ REPAIR ↔ RECURRENCE.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Maturana’s source unit is a relational flow of consensual coordinations, not an isolated sign.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
OPERATIVE_FORCE(p_t) = f(p_t, interaction_history, current_world, response, later_repair).

TENSION:
LLMs are not living autopoietic organisms, and Maturana’s account is biological and relational.

MISSING:
A principled criterion for when “languaging” may be extended to human-machine coupling without category error.

BOUNDARY:
Do not claim the model itself languages in Maturana’s full biological sense merely because dialogue is recursive.

CITATION TRAIL:
Maturana 2002 → language-games → thick prompting → conversational agents.

TEST:
Compare isolated prompt evaluation with interaction-sequence evaluation; determine how much behavior becomes predictable only after including repair history.

PLATFORM:
Biology of cognition / conversational AI.

LINKS:
ZET-LANGUAGING; ZET-PROMPT-THICKNESS; ZET-LANGUAGE-GAME; ZET-WORLD-TO-WORLD.

BIBTEX:
@article{Maturana2002Autopoiesis, author={Humberto Maturana Romesin}, title={Autopoiesis, Structural Coupling and Cognition}, journal={Cybernetics and Human Knowing}, year={2002}, volume={9}, number={3-4}, pages={5--34}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-MATURANA-OBJECTS-AS-RECURSIVE-COORDINATIONS-2002

TITLE:
Objects Arise as Operations Around Which Further Coordination Occurs

SOURCE:
Humberto Maturana Romesín - “Autopoiesis, Structural Coupling and Cognition” - 2002. ([Reflexus](https://reflexus.org/wp-content/uploads/Autopoiesis-structural-coupling-and-cognition.pdf))

Maturana says objects arise in languaging as operations of coordination around which further doings are recursively coordinated.

objects; operations; shared objects; inter-objectivity; recursive coordination; domains of reality.

An object can function as a node for continued coordination without requiring one final representation that alone constitutes the object.

Can the yellow circle remain one shared object while different programming languages operationalize it through incompatible local ontologies?

What has to remain recurrently coordinatable for heterogeneous representations to count as one object?

Repeated coordinated distinctions stabilize an entity that subsequently becomes available as the object of further coordinated action.

Objects belong to domains of shared entities generated in languaging.
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-MATURANA-ORGANIZATION-STRUCTURE-2002

TITLE:
Identity Persists Through Structural Change Only While Organization Is Conserved

SOURCE:
Humberto Maturana Romesín - “Autopoiesis, Structural Coupling and Cognition” - 2002. ([Reflexus](https://reflexus.org/wp-content/uploads/Autopoiesis-structural-coupling-and-cognition.pdf))

PASSAGE:
Maturana distinguishes changing structure from conserved organization; class identity persists only while organization is conserved.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
Transformation invariants and artifact identity.

LOCAL MOVE:
Sharpens the eigenform branch by separating what may vary from what must remain conserved, while explicitly marking application to graphic objects as analogical.

SOURCE TERMS:
organization; structure; class identity; conservation; change of state; disintegration.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
Identity can be defined by relations conserved through transformation rather than by preservation of material components.

QUESTION:
What is the “organization” of YELLOW-CIRCLE as distinct from one particular raster or representation?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can prompt-based editing discover an artifact’s organization empirically by perturbing structure until observers withdraw identity judgments?

MECHANISM:
Structural transformations are tolerated while the relations constituting class identity remain conserved.

FORMAL SHIFT:
IDENTITY = SAME REPRESENTATION → IDENTITY = CONSERVED ORGANIZATION ACROSS REPRESENTATIONAL CHANGE.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Maturana’s organization/structure distinction concerns systems and is centrally developed for living systems.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
STRUCTURE = concrete pixels/code/trajectory.
ORGANIZATION = relations whose conservation supports classification as YELLOW-CIRCLE.

TENSION:
Graphic objects are not autopoietic systems; transferring this distinction requires justification.

MISSING:
A non-biological account showing when Maturana intended the organization/structure distinction to apply generally.

BOUNDARY:
This zettel does not claim that a rendered circle possesses biological organization.

CITATION TRAIL:
Maturana 2002 → eigenform → permissible transformations → Yellow Circle.

TEST:
Systematically vary size, position, hue, edge quality, topology, and representation; record the boundary at which observers cease preserving CIRCLE and YELLOW-CIRCLE identity.

PLATFORM:
Cybernetics / experimental semantics.

LINKS:
ZET-EIGENFORM; ZET-YELLOW-CIRCLE; ZET-ASPECT-INSTABILITY.
```
