```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-MATURANA-EXPLANATION-NOT-REDUCTION-2002

TITLE:
A Generative Explanation Does Not Collapse the Explained Phenomenon Into Its Mechanism

SOURCE:
Humberto Maturana Romesín - “Autopoiesis, Structural Coupling and Cognition” - 2002. ([Reflexus](https://reflexus.org/wp-content/uploads/Autopoiesis-structural-coupling-and-cognition.pdf))

PASSAGE:
Maturana states explicitly that explanations “do not constitute, and cannot constitute, phenomenal reductions.”

RESEARCH OBJECT:
The stopping rule for operational audits.

LOCAL MOVE:
Blocks the assumption that descending from prompt to API to rasterizer to pixels reveals progressively “truer” versions of the circle.

SOURCE TERMS:
explanation; generative mechanism; operational domains; phenomenal reduction; observer.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
Recovering the mechanism that generates an artifact does not automatically replace the higher-level phenomenon with that mechanism.

QUESTION:
Where should an operational audit stop if no lower implementation layer possesses privileged ontological status?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can the audit preserve generative dependence without turning dependence into reduction?

MECHANISM:
Observer-accepted generative mechanisms can explain phenomena while mechanism and phenomenon occupy distinct operational domains.

FORMAL SHIFT:
LOWER LEVEL = TRUE OBJECT → LOWER LEVEL = ONE GENERATIVE DOMAIN RELATED TO ANOTHER.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Explanations propose generative mechanisms without requiring phenomenal reduction.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
TRACE representation boundaries until the research question no longer changes, not until a supposedly final ontological substrate is reached.

TENSION:
Operational explanation still requires causal adequacy; anti-reduction is not permission to treat all descriptions as equally explanatory.

MISSING:
A precise audit stopping criterion based on explanatory gain.

BOUNDARY:
Maturana’s philosophical account does not itself specify software tracing methodology.

CITATION TRAIL:
Maturana 2002 → operational audit → Yellow Circle stack.

TEST:
For each added implementation layer, ask whether it changes the set of possible failure explanations or experimental predictions; stop when further layers only redescribe already-accounted causal relations.

PLATFORM:
Philosophy of explanation / software studies.

LINKS:
ZET-YELLOW-CIRCLE; ZET-OPERATIVE-DESCRIPTION; ZET-WORLD-TO-WORLD; ZET-CHINDOGU-YELLOW-CIRCLE.

BIBTEX:
@article{Maturana2002Autopoiesis, author={Humberto Maturana Romesin}, title={Autopoiesis, Structural Coupling and Cognition}, journal={Cybernetics and Human Knowing}, year={2002}, volume={9}, number={3-4}, pages={5--34}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-ASHBY-REQUISITE-ACCESS-1956

TITLE:
Requisite Variety Must Reach the Regulator Through an Effective Channel

SOURCE:
W. Ross Ashby - An Introduction to Cybernetics - 1956. ([Ashby Info](https://ashby.info/Ashby-Introduction-to-Cybernetics.pdf))

PASSAGE:
Ashby states that regulatory capacity is limited by the regulator’s capacity as a communication channel.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
A temporal-access qualification to the proposed Law of Requisite Description.

LOCAL MOVE:
Corrects the weaker claim that requisite distinctions merely need to “exist somewhere” in the coupled system. They must become available to the regulating operation in usable form.

SOURCE TERMS:
requisite variety; disturbance; regulator; channel of communication; outcome; correction channel.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
A repository, screenshot, prior message, or tool schema can contain the needed distinction yet fail to regulate behavior if the agent cannot retrieve it when deciding.

QUESTION:
When does environmental information count as operative context rather than merely available information?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Is prompt thickness better defined by accessible requisite distinction at decision time than by total contextual information?

MECHANISM:
A disturbance can be countered only if distinctions sufficient to select the relevant regulatory response pass through an effective channel.

FORMAL SHIFT:
DESCRIPTION EXISTS → DESCRIPTION IS ACCESSIBLE BEFORE ACTION.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Ashby relates regulatory variety to disturbance variety and communication-channel capacity.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
For each action-relevant disturbance class d, the discriminating information must be recoverable by the controller before the response deadline.

TENSION:
Ashby’s mathematical variety is not identical to semantic “description thickness.”

MISSING:
A principled mapping from contextual distinctions to measurable information variety in agentic interfaces.

BOUNDARY:
“Law of Requisite Description” remains our extension, not Ashby’s term.

CITATION TRAIL:
Ashby 1956 → requisite variety → proposed requisite description → progressive context disclosure.

TEST:
Keep total stored context fixed while varying whether the relevant detail is directly visible, retrievable by tool call, or inaccessible until after action; compare regulation success.

PLATFORM:
Cybernetics / agent context.

LINKS:
ZET-LAW-REQUISITE-DESCRIPTION; ZET-PROMPT-THICKNESS; ZET-DESCRIPTION-MIGRATION.

BIBTEX:
@book{Ashby1956Cybernetics, author={W. Ross Ashby}, title={An Introduction to Cybernetics}, year={1956}, publisher={Chapman and Hall}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-CONANT-ASHBY-GOOD-REGULATOR-BOUNDARY-1970

TITLE:
The Good Regulator Theorem Is Conditional, Not a License to Call Every Successful Prompt a World Model

SOURCE:
Roger C. Conant and W. Ross Ashby - “Every Good Regulator of a System Must Be a Model of That System” - 1970. ([Governance Foundation](https://governance.foundation/assets/frameworks/other/Conant_Ashby%20Every%20Good%20Regulator%20of%20a%20system%20must%20be%20a%20model%20of%20that%20system.pdf))

PASSAGE:
The theorem concerns regulators maximally both successful and simple “under very broad conditions”; the paper notes that exact assumptions are specified.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
The boundary of the model-of-world claim in world-to-world prompting.

LOCAL MOVE:
Prevents overextension of the Good Regulator theorem into a universal claim that every successful agent must explicitly represent the full system it acts upon.

SOURCE TERMS:
regulator; model; isomorphism; maximally successful; simple; exact assumptions.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
The memorable slogan is stronger than what may safely be transferred outside the theorem’s formal assumptions.

QUESTION:
Which assumptions of the theorem are satisfied by an LLM acting through a GUI?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can successful world-to-world transformation rely on partial, transient, or action-specific models rather than a globally isomorphic world model?

MECHANISM:
Under the theorem’s specified conditions, optimal regulation imposes a model relation between regulator and regulated system.

FORMAL SHIFT:
EVERY SUCCESSFUL AGENT HAS A WORLD MODEL → CERTAIN OPTIMAL REGULATORS REQUIRE A MODEL RELATION UNDER DEFINED CONDITIONS.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Conant and Ashby prove a formal theorem with stated assumptions.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
Treat “model adequacy” as an empirical variable indexed to task and disturbance set rather than a universal property.

TENSION:
The world-to-world framework currently risks using “model” too loosely.

MISSING:
Formal comparison between theorem variables and agentic GUI interaction variables.

BOUNDARY:
Do not cite the Good Regulator theorem as proof that contemporary foundation models contain human-like internal world models.

CITATION TRAIL:
Conant and Ashby 1970 → requisite description → world-to-world model.

TEST:
Map an agent task onto the theorem’s exact variables and assumptions before invoking it; record which assumptions fail.

PLATFORM:
Cybernetics / AI theory.

LINKS:
ZET-LAW-REQUISITE-DESCRIPTION; ZET-WORLD-TO-WORLD; ZET-MODEL-OF-MODEL-FOR.

BIBTEX:
@article{ConantAshby1970GoodRegulator, author={Roger C. Conant and W. Ross Ashby}, title={Every Good Regulator of a System Must Be a Model of That System}, journal={International Journal of Systems Science}, year={1970}, volume={1}, number={2}, pages={89--97}, doi={10.1080/00207727008920220}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-GREEN-BLACKWELL-INTERACTION-LANGUAGE-1998

TITLE:
Cognitive Dimensions Already Treats Interactive Use as an Interaction Language

SOURCE:
Thomas R. G. Green and Alan F. Blackwell - Cognitive Dimensions of Information Artefacts: A Tutorial - 1998. ([Cambridge Computer Lab](https://www.cl.cam.ac.uk/~afb21/CognitiveDimensions/CDtutorial.pdf))

PASSAGE:
The tutorial models interactive artefacts as controlled by an “interaction language” whose commands construct or modify information structures.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
The genealogy of GUI-as-language and attention tax.

LOCAL MOVE:
Moves “GUI as spatialized language” from metaphor toward an existing HCI formal vocabulary.

SOURCE TERMS:
interaction language; notation; information artefact; commands; persistence; environment.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
HCI already had a framework in which using an interactive device could be analyzed as uttering commands in a language without reducing interaction to textual syntax.

QUESTION:
What does “spatialized language” add beyond Green and Blackwell’s interaction-language account?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can the Yellow Circle unify programming notation and direct manipulation using one cross-medium grammar of operative distinctions?

MECHANISM:
Interactive actions function as commands whose effects become persistent information structures.

FORMAL SHIFT:
GUI ≠ LANGUAGE → GUI HAS AN INTERACTION LANGUAGE WHOSE EXPRESSIONS MAY BE GESTURAL/SPATIAL.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Cognitive Dimensions models notation plus environment and applies dimensions to the system as a whole.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
GUI grammar distributes syntax across spatial placement, state, tool selection, gesture, and persistence.

TENSION:
“Attention tax” risks redescribing existing Cognitive Dimensions without adding explanatory power.

MISSING:
A direct comparison showing what attention tax predicts that Cognitive Dimensions does not.

BOUNDARY:
Cognitive Dimensions is explicitly a broad-brush usability framework, not a cognitive-process theory.

CITATION TRAIL:
Green and Blackwell 1998 → GUI language → attention tax → Yellow Circle.

TEST:
Score SVG, Logo, and Paint with canonical Cognitive Dimensions and with the proposed attention-distribution variables; retain new terminology only where it captures distinct variance.

PLATFORM:
HCI / notation studies.

LINKS:
ZET-GUI-SPATIAL-LANGUAGE; ZET-ATTENTION-TAX; ZET-YELLOW-CIRCLE.

BIBTEX:
@techreport{GreenBlackwell1998CognitiveDimensions, author={Thomas R. G. Green and Alan F. Blackwell}, title={Cognitive Dimensions of Information Artefacts: A Tutorial}, year={1998}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-GREEN-BLACKWELL-SECONDARY-NOTATION-1998

TITLE:
Operative Thickness Can Reside Outside Formal Syntax

SOURCE:
Thomas R. G. Green and Alan F. Blackwell - Cognitive Dimensions of Information Artefacts: A Tutorial - 1998. ([Cambridge Computer Lab](https://www.cl.cam.ac.uk/~afb21/CognitiveDimensions/CDtutorial.pdf))

PASSAGE:
The tutorial notes that traditional notations routinely carry redundant information by means other than formal syntax, including whitespace, grouping, and layout.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
Secondary notation as a precursor to distributed prompt thickness.

LOCAL MOVE:
Provides a source-level distinction between formal syntax and information carried by presentation, directly pressuring any equation of prompt with explicit text.

SOURCE TERMS:
secondary notation; formal syntax; grouping; layout; redundant information; escape from formalism.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
Information excluded from the official grammar can still be central to human parsing, checking, and successful action.

QUESTION:
When contextual information becomes directly machine-actionable, is it still “secondary notation,” or has the boundary of the language expanded?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Does multimodal prompting erase the primary/secondary notation distinction by allowing gesture, layout, image, and interface state to become executable?

MECHANISM:
Additional distinctions are encoded in presentation channels not defined by the formal syntax.

FORMAL SHIFT:
PROMPT TEXT + CONTEXT → MULTICHANNEL OPERATIVE DESCRIPTION.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Secondary notation carries information outside the official syntax.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
THICKNESS = recoverable task-relevant distinctions across official syntax + secondary notation + world state + interaction history.

TENSION:
Secondary notation was developed for information artefacts and human interpretation; multimodal agents may operationalize those channels differently.

MISSING:
Empirical cases where changing only secondary notation changes an agent’s executable interpretation while semantic text remains constant.

BOUNDARY:
Do not collapse every contextual cue into “notation”; some cues belong to environment or history rather than inscription.

CITATION TRAIL:
Green and Blackwell 1998 → thick prompting → description migration.

TEST:
Keep prompt words fixed while independently perturbing layout, pointer selection, highlighted object, screen position, and code surroundings.

PLATFORM:
Notation / multimodal prompting.

LINKS:
ZET-PROMPT-THICKNESS; ZET-DESCRIPTION-MIGRATION; ZET-GUI-SPATIAL-LANGUAGE.

BIBTEX:
@techreport{GreenBlackwell1998CognitiveDimensions, author={Thomas R. G. Green and Alan F. Blackwell}, title={Cognitive Dimensions of Information Artefacts: A Tutorial}, year={1998}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-GREEN-BLACKWELL-PREMATURE-COMMITMENT-1998

TITLE:
Requirements Can Force Decisions Before the Information Needed to Make Them Exists

SOURCE:
Thomas R. G. Green and Alan F. Blackwell - Cognitive Dimensions of Information Artefacts: A Tutorial - 1998. ([Cambridge Computer Lab](https://www.cl.cam.ac.uk/~afb21/CognitiveDimensions/CDtutorial.pdf))

PASSAGE:
Premature commitment occurs when an ordering constraint forces a decision before the information needed for that decision is available.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
The software requirements problem as a notation-induced temporal error.

LOCAL MOVE:
Replaces the loose Jobs maxim “users do not know what they want until they see it” with an established representational mechanism: the design process may demand distinctions too early.

SOURCE TERMS:
premature commitment; enforced lookahead; natural sequence; medium; information availability; technical design.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
“Incomplete requirements” can be a property of the representation schedule rather than a deficiency in the user.

QUESTION:
Which software requirements become knowable only after an artifact or consequence exists?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can generative interaction reduce premature commitment by making specification and execution interleave?

MECHANISM:
A notation or process requires an irreversible or costly choice before later evidence capable of discriminating that choice is available.

FORMAL SHIFT:
REQUIREMENTS FIRST → PROVISIONAL DESCRIPTION ↔ ARTIFACT ↔ REVISED DESCRIPTION.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Green and Blackwell treat premature commitment as a Cognitive Dimension affected by notation, environment, and medium.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
If distinction d becomes observable only after prototype state W₁, requiring d in specification for W₀ imposes premature descriptive commitment.

TENSION:
Iterative generative systems can also create new forms of premature commitment by silently inferring an underspecified choice and propagating it.

MISSING:
Comparative evidence on when model inference reduces versus conceals premature commitment.

BOUNDARY:
Not every requirement uncertainty is a notation problem.

CITATION TRAIL:
Cognitive Dimensions → HCD requirements → thick prompting → critique loop.

TEST:
For a Yellow Circle variant with intentionally unresolved size, compare upfront complete specification against interactive “circle there → bigger → there” refinement; count commitments made before evidence.

PLATFORM:
HCI / requirements engineering.

LINKS:
ZET-HCD-AS-TRANSLATION; ZET-THICK-PROMPTING; ZET-CRITICISM-AS-PROGRAMMING; ZET-WORLD-TO-WORLD.

BIBTEX:
@techreport{GreenBlackwell1998CognitiveDimensions, author={Thomas R. G. Green and Alan F. Blackwell}, title={Cognitive Dimensions of Information Artefacts: A Tutorial}, year={1998}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-NAUR-PROGRAM-IS-NOT-THEORY-1985

TITLE:
The Program Text Is Not the Primary Object of Programming

SOURCE:
Peter Naur - “Programming as Theory Building” - 1985. ([Naur](https://www.naur.com/comp/c1-4.html))

PASSAGE:
Naur’s abstract makes the primary aim not program production but programmers building theories of how problems are solved by program execution.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
Whether a codebase can function as a complete operative description of its own continuation.

LOCAL MOVE:
Opposes the strong claim that “the codebase is the thick prompt.” On Naur’s account, the operative theory may exceed the artefacts left behind.

SOURCE TERMS:
theory building; programmer; program execution; modification; changing demands.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
A repository can display enormous regularity yet still fail to preserve the theory that made its design choices intelligible.

QUESTION:
When an agent successfully modifies unfamiliar code from repository context alone, has it reconstructed the missing theory or merely found a locally adequate continuation?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can model-scale prior training partially substitute for the team-held theory Naur thought essential to program life?

MECHANISM:
Modification depends on an internalized theory of how the domain and program solution fit, not merely reproduction of textual patterns.

FORMAL SHIFT:
CODEBASE = OPERATIVE DESCRIPTION → CODEBASE = EVIDENCE FROM WHICH OPERATIVE THEORY MAY OR MAY NOT BE RECONSTRUCTED.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Programming as theory building.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
REPOSITORY_CONTEXT + MODEL_PRIORS → inferred local theory → modification.
Successful patch does not prove full theory recovery.

TENSION:
Modern foundation models create a reconstructive capacity unavailable in Naur’s historical setting.

MISSING:
Experiments comparing model success on repositories with identical source but differing undocumented design rationale.

BOUNDARY:
Do not attribute to Naur claims about LLMs or prompt context.

CITATION TRAIL:
Naur 1985 → description migration → Claude Code context → implied reader.

TEST:
Create two codebases with surface-similar patterns but different hidden design rationales; ask an agent to extend both without rationale, then provide rationale and compare failures.

PLATFORM:
Software engineering / agentic coding.

LINKS:
ZET-DESCRIPTION-MIGRATION; ZET-IMPLIED-USER; ZET-CLAUDE-DESCRIPTION-MIGRATION-2026.

BIBTEX:
@article{Naur1985TheoryBuilding, author={Peter Naur}, title={Programming as Theory Building}, journal={Microprocessing and Microprogramming}, year={1985}, volume={15}, number={5}, pages={253--261}, doi={10.1016/0165-6074(85)90032-8}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-WINOGRAD-COORDINATOR-DECLARES-FORCE-1987

TITLE:
The Coordinator Makes Conversational Force Explicit Before the Message Proceeds

SOURCE:
Terry Winograd - “A Language/Action Perspective on the Design of Cooperative Work” - 1987. ([LRI](https://www.lri.fr/~mbl/ENS/CSCW/2016/papers/Winograd-HCI87.pdf))

PASSAGE:
The Coordinator presents distinct openings such as Request and Offer, with templates that structure the subsequent conversation.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
The difference between classifying language action in advance and allowing its force to stabilize through interaction.

LOCAL MOVE:
Corrects the idea that The Coordinator resembles contemporary LLM intent inference. It makes users enter an explicit communicative-action ontology.

SOURCE TERMS:
Request; Offer; conversation for action; commitments; language/action perspective; templates.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
A theory intended to treat language as action becomes a GUI in which the user must classify the action before fully performing it.

QUESTION:
What conversational flexibility is gained or lost by declaring illocutionary force before the utterance?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Could generative interfaces reopen the ambiguity that workflow systems had to eliminate in order to compute coordination?

MECHANISM:
The interface constrains possible conversational trajectories by requiring selection among predefined action categories.

FORMAL SHIFT:
LANGUAGE AS EMERGENT COORDINATION → LANGUAGE ACTION AS DECLARED WORKFLOW STATE.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
The Coordinator structures communication around action categories and expected responses.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
UTTERANCE = FORCE_ENUM + CONTENT + COMMITMENT_STATE.

TENSION:
The language/action perspective was explicitly anti-information-transfer in orientation, yet implementation may harden its action categories.

MISSING:
Winograd’s 1994 response to Suchman and the degree to which later Language/Action work relaxed these categories.

BOUNDARY:
Do not reduce the whole Winograd/Flores project to one software product.

CITATION TRAIL:
Maturana → Winograd/Flores → Coordinator → Suchman 1994.

TEST:
Compare a task in two interfaces: one requires REQUEST/OFFER/PROMISE selection before text; one allows free-form interaction and infers/negotiates force after response.

PLATFORM:
CSCW / language-action HCI.

LINKS:
ZET-LANGUAGING; ZET-LANGUAGE-ACTION; ZET-GUI-SPATIAL-LANGUAGE; ZET-SUCHMAN-CATEGORIES-POLITICS-1994.

BIBTEX:
@article{Winograd1987LanguageAction, author={Terry Winograd}, title={A Language/Action Perspective on the Design of Cooperative Work}, journal={Human-Computer Interaction}, year={1987}, volume={3}, number={1}, pages={3--30}, doi={10.1207/s15327051hci0301_2}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-SUCHMAN-CATEGORIES-POLITICS-1994

TITLE:
Making Intent Machine-Legible Can Turn Description Into Discipline

SOURCE:
Lucy Suchman - “Do Categories Have Politics? The Language/Action Perspective Reconsidered” - 1994. ([Lancaster University](https://www.lancaster.ac.uk/fass/doc_library/sociology/Suchman_categories_1994.pdf))

PASSAGE:
Suchman characterizes The Coordinator as technological “intention-accounting” and questions the disciplinary consequences of categorizing speakers’ intent.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
The politics of turning language-games into computable categories.

LOCAL MOVE:
Adds a political failure mode to thick prompting: increasing machine legibility may force situated human action into the system’s preferred ontology.

SOURCE TERMS:
categorization; intent; discipline; control; coordination technologies; intention-accounting.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
A representation designed to support coordination can reshape the coordination by requiring participants to inhabit its categories.

QUESTION:
When does an operative description support a human practice, and when does it prescribe the practice it claims merely to represent?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Does LLM-based ambiguity tolerance genuinely reduce categorical discipline, or merely hide classification inside inaccessible inference?

MECHANISM:
A system makes selected action categories obligatory or consequential; participants adapt their conduct to those categories.

FORMAL SHIFT:
MODEL OF COMMUNICATION → INTERFACE CATEGORY → MODEL FOR COMMUNICATION.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Suchman critiques speech-act categories as political features of CSCW design.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
CATEGORY becomes operative when access to next actions depends on passing through it.

TENSION:
Removing explicit categories can decrease visible constraint while increasing opaque model-mediated categorization.

MISSING:
Comparative study of explicit workflow categories versus latent LLM intent classification.

BOUNDARY:
Suchman’s argument is institutional and political, not simply a usability critique.

CITATION TRAIL:
Winograd 1987 → Suchman 1994 → model-of/model-for → human-centered translation.

TEST:
Hold a coordination task constant; compare explicit categorical workflow, free-form deterministic parser, and LLM-mediated interaction; document who can inspect, contest, or revise the operative categories.

PLATFORM:
CSCW / STS / HCI.

LINKS:
ZET-WINOGRAD-COORDINATOR-DECLARES-FORCE-1987; ZET-HCD-AS-TRANSLATION; ZET-MODEL-OF-MODEL-FOR; ZET-IMPLIED-USER.

BIBTEX:
@article{Suchman1994Categories, author={Lucy Suchman}, title={Do Categories Have Politics? The Language/Action Perspective Reconsidered}, journal={Computer Supported Cooperative Work}, year={1994}, volume={2}, pages={177--190}, doi={10.1007/BF00749015}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-BENJAMIN-MODE-OF-INTENTION-1923

TITLE:
Translation Can Preserve an Intended Object While Changing the Mode of Intending It

SOURCE:
Walter Benjamin - “The Translator’s Task” / “The Task of the Translator.” ([Germanic Languages & Literatures](https://german.yale.edu/sites/default/files/benjamin_translators_task.pdf))

PASSAGE:
Benjamin distinguishes the intended object from the language-specific mode in which that object is intended.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
Cross-language Yellow Circle implementations as transformations of operational intention rather than equivalent encodings.

LOCAL MOVE:
Refines the polyglot circle experiment: Logo, SVG, GLSL, and deixis may converge on a recognizable artifact while construing what it is to specify that artifact differently.

SOURCE TERMS:
intended object; mode of intention; kinship; translation; language.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
Equivalent outputs do not imply equivalent ways of articulating or organizing the object.

QUESTION:
What survives translation across programming languages besides visible output?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can a computational translation be evaluated for whether it transforms the receiving language rather than simply compiling into its pre-existing ontology?

MECHANISM:
Languages partition and articulate a shared target through non-identical systems of distinctions.

FORMAL SHIFT:
SAME MEANING IN DIFFERENT CODE → SAME TARGET, DIFFERENT MODE OF OPERATIONAL INTENTION.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Benjamin contrasts intended object with mode of intention in literary-linguistic translation.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
SVG intends circle as object attributes;
Logo as movement history;
GLSL as point-membership predicate;
deixis as situated selection/action.

TENSION:
Benjamin’s theory is literary, historical, and bound to his larger philosophy of “pure language”; computational equivalence is not his subject.

MISSING:
A criterion for distinguishing a genuinely transformed receiving formalism from ordinary compilation.

BOUNDARY:
Do not treat visible artifact identity as Benjamin’s “pure language.”

CITATION TRAIL:
Benjamin → Yellow Circle polyglot corpus → description migration → worldtexting.

TEST:
Translate “put a yellow circle there” into a target language twice: first by forcing existing primitives, second by extending the target with a new deictic primitive; compare what distinctions the target subsequently makes available.

PLATFORM:
Translation theory / programming languages.

LINKS:
ZET-TASK-OF-TRANSLATOR; ZET-YELLOW-CIRCLE; ZET-DESCRIPTION-MIGRATION; ZET-GUI-SPATIAL-LANGUAGE.

BIBTEX:
@incollection{Benjamin1923Translator, author={Walter Benjamin}, title={The Task of the Translator}, year={1923}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-BENJAMIN-RECEIVER-REFUSAL-1923

TITLE:
Benjamin Is a Bad Source for the Implied User Because He Explicitly Rejects the Receiver as the Basis of Art

SOURCE:
Walter Benjamin - “The Translator’s Task” / “The Task of the Translator.” ([Germanic Languages & Literatures](https://german.yale.edu/sites/default/files/benjamin_translators_task.pdf))

PASSAGE:
Benjamin rejects the ideal receiver as a useful theoretical foundation for understanding artworks.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
The genealogy of the implied-reader / implied-user branch.

LOCAL MOVE:
Kills a misleading edge in the current graph: Benjamin should ground mode-of-intention and transformation of the receiving language, not the implied-user claim.

SOURCE TERMS:
receiver; ideal receiver; theory of art; translation.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
The same essay useful for translation actively resists the audience-centered move we were trying to attach to it.

QUESTION:
Which sources should ground the software “implied user” instead?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Does software differ from Benjamin’s artwork precisely because a competent receiver can be constitutive of execution rather than incidental to the work’s existence?

MECHANISM:
Source correction by separating adjacent but incompatible theoretical claims.

FORMAL SHIFT:
BARTHES/BENJAMIN → IMPLIED USER becomes ISER / ECO / WOOLGAR / AKRICH → IMPLIED USER.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Benjamin’s opening argument brackets receiver-oriented aesthetics.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
For software, infer IMPLIED_OPERATOR from competencies required to produce valid continuations; ground this elsewhere.

TENSION:
Interactive software may make the receiver operationally constitutive in a way Benjamin’s account of art intentionally excludes.

MISSING:
Primary-source forage on Iser’s implied reader, Eco’s model reader, Woolgar’s configuring the user, and Akrich’s scripts.

BOUNDARY:
This zettel corrects genealogy; it does not invalidate the broader claim that software presupposes users.

CITATION TRAIL:
Benjamin → previous implied-reader analogy → genealogy correction.

TEST:
Replace Benjamin citations in the implied-user branch and determine whether the argument survives on HCI/reader-response sources alone.

PLATFORM:
Genealogy correction.

LINKS:
ZET-IMPLIED-USER; ZET-BENJAMIN-MODE-OF-INTENTION-1923; ZET-HCD-AS-TRANSLATION.

BIBTEX:
@incollection{Benjamin1923Translator, author={Walter Benjamin}, title={The Task of the Translator}, year={1923}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-ANSCOMBE-MISTAKE-LOCATION-1957

TITLE:
Before “Direction of Fit,” Anscombe’s Shopping Lists Locate Where the Mistake Lies

SOURCE:
G. E. M. Anscombe - Intention - §32; text reproduced by Stanford Encyclopedia of Philosophy. ([Stanford Encyclopedia of Philosophy](https://plato.stanford.edu/archives/sum2016/entries/anscombe/))

PASSAGE:
When shopper and list diverge, the mistake lies in performance; when detective and record diverge, the mistake lies in the record.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
A genealogy correction for direction-of-fit claims in generative critique.

LOCAL MOVE:
Replaces the over-clean attribution “Anscombe’s direction of fit” with her actual distinction between kinds of error relation.

SOURCE TERMS:
shopping list; order; intention; record; mistake; performance.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
The same propositional contents can stand in distinct practical relations to what occurs without Anscombe needing the later phrase “direction of fit.”

QUESTION:
Does “That’s not a circle” locate a mistake in the artifact, the description, the interpreter, or the shared criterion?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can agentic criticism be classified by where the interaction assigns responsibility for mismatch?

MECHANISM:
The practical role of a representation determines which side is revised when representation and event diverge.

FORMAL SHIFT:
DIRECTION OF FIT AS SLOGAN → LOCATION OF CORRECTABLE ERROR IN A PRACTICE.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Anscombe’s list/order/record comparison.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
Mismatch(prompt, artifact) + language-game → revise artifact | revise description | revise interpretation | revise criterion.

TENSION:
Later speech-act theory generalizes Anscombe into direction-of-fit terminology that can obscure her action-theoretic context. ([Stanford Encyclopedia of Philosophy](https://plato.stanford.edu/archives/sum2010/entries/speech-acts/))

MISSING:
Primary genealogy from Anscombe §32 through Searle’s formal “direction of fit” terminology.

BOUNDARY:
Do not state that Anscombe coined “direction of fit.”

CITATION TRAIL:
Anscombe 1957 → later speech-act theory → criticism-as-programming.

TEST:
Classify correction turns in a Yellow Circle session by which component participants actually revise after mismatch.

PLATFORM:
Action theory / generative interaction.

LINKS:
ZET-DIRECTION-OF-FIT; ZET-CRITICISM-AS-PROGRAMMING; ZET-MODEL-OF-MODEL-FOR.

BIBTEX:
@book{Anscombe1957Intention, author={G. E. M. Anscombe}, title={Intention}, year={1957}, publisher={Basil Blackwell}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-CLAUDE-DESCRIPTION-MIGRATION-2026

TITLE:
System-Prompt Shrinkage Can Coincide With a Larger Operative Context

SOURCE:
Thariq Shihipar - “The New Rules of Context Engineering for Claude 5 Generation Models” - Claude by Anthropic - 24 July 2026. ([Claude](https://claude.com/blog/the-new-rules-of-context-engineering-for-claude-5-generation-models?reference=FUZZ))

PASSAGE:
Anthropic reports removing over 80% of Claude Code’s system prompt for newer models with no measurable loss on its coding evaluations.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
Description migration from explicit instructions into competence and surrounding context.

LOCAL MOVE:
Provides direct contemporary evidence against identifying operative description with system-prompt length.

SOURCE TERMS:
context engineering; system prompt; Skills; CLAUDE.md; memory; overconstraining; judgment.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
A thinner explicit instruction layer can accompany equal measured task performance because more distinctions are recoverable elsewhere.

QUESTION:
Which removed instructions became unnecessary because of model capability, and which were relocated into other context surfaces?

DEEPER QUESTION:
How should prompt thickness be measured when the operative description is distributed and dynamically retrieved?

MECHANISM:
Model capability plus repository, memory, skills, and interface context can substitute for previously explicit guidance.

FORMAL SHIFT:
PROMPT LENGTH → DISTRIBUTED OPERATIVE CONTEXT.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Anthropic distinguishes user prompt from context assembled from system prompt, Skills, CLAUDE.md, memory, and other sources.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
T_oper = explicit + latent competence + environment + retrieved context + interaction history.

TENSION:
“No measurable loss” is relative to Anthropic’s evaluation suite and does not prove every removed distinction is universally irrelevant.

MISSING:
The removed prompt versions and per-instruction ablation data.

BOUNDARY:
Do not claim Claude Code autonomously rewrites its own system prompt every two weeks; the official source does not establish that.

CITATION TRAIL:
Claude context engineering 2026 → description migration → requisite description → prompt thickness.

TEST:
Replay a fixed coding suite under old and reduced contexts while independently withholding repository cues, memory, tools, and skills to locate where performance-supporting distinctions moved.

PLATFORM:
Agentic coding / context engineering.

LINKS:
ZET-DESCRIPTION-MIGRATION; ZET-PROMPT-THICKNESS; ZET-LAW-REQUISITE-DESCRIPTION; ZET-NAUR-PROGRAM-IS-NOT-THEORY-1985.

BIBTEX:
@misc{Shihipar2026ContextEngineering, author={Thariq Shihipar}, title={The New Rules of Context Engineering for Claude 5 Generation Models}, year={2026}, howpublished={Claude by Anthropic}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-CLAUDE-PROMPT-ABLATION-2026

TITLE:
Prompt Lines Can Be Tested for Operative Force by Removing Them and Measuring Behavioral Change

SOURCE:
Anthropic - “An Update on Recent Claude Code Quality Reports” - 23 April 2026. ([Anthropic](https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/april-23-postmortem))

PASSAGE:
Anthropic reports that a system instruction intended to reduce verbosity harmed coding quality and was reverted.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
A causal test for operative description.

LOCAL MOVE:
Turns “difference that makes a difference” into an actionable prompt-research protocol: remove or perturb a descriptive element and measure task-relevant behavioral change.

SOURCE TERMS:
system prompt; coding quality; evaluation; revert; quality report; reasoning effort.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
More instruction can reduce capability; descriptive thickness is therefore not monotonically improved by adding rules.

QUESTION:
When does added description increase requisite discrimination, and when does it overconstrain competent inference?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can prompt engineering be reconstructed as experimental causal analysis rather than preservation of successful verbal rituals?

MECHANISM:
An instruction changes the model’s behavioral policy; evaluation detects whether that change helps or harms task performance.

FORMAL SHIFT:
DESCRIPTION PRESENT → DESCRIPTION OPERATIVE IFF PERTURBATION CHANGES RELEVANT BEHAVIOR.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Anthropic traces degradation to specific product/context changes and reverts harmful changes.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
d is locally operative under model M, context C, task T, evaluator E if intervention on d changes E(M,C,T).

TENSION:
A null result can mean genuine irrelevance or an insensitive evaluator.

MISSING:
Evaluator-robust ablation methods and repeated-run confidence intervals for stochastic models.

BOUNDARY:
Operative force is relational to model, context, task, and evaluator; it is not an intrinsic property of a sentence.

CITATION TRAIL:
Anthropic postmortem 2026 → Bateson → prompt superstition → requisite description.

TEST:
Run factorial perturbations of canonical prompt rituals with multiple semantic and behavioral evaluators; separate model effects from evaluator artifacts.

PLATFORM:
Prompt science / evaluation.

LINKS:
ZET-PROMPT-SUPERSTITION; ZET-OPERATIVE-RITUAL; ZET-DIFFERENCE-MAKES-DIFFERENCE; ZET-CLAUDE-DESCRIPTION-MIGRATION-2026.

BIBTEX:
@misc{Anthropic2026ClaudeCodePostmortem, author={{Anthropic}}, title={An Update on Recent Claude Code Quality Reports}, year={2026}, howpublished={Anthropic Engineering}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-CONTEXT-PROGRESSIVE-DISCLOSURE-2026

TITLE:
Thick Context Need Not Be Present Up Front If Requisite Distinctions Are Retrievable When Needed

SOURCE:
Thariq Shihipar - “The New Rules of Context Engineering for Claude 5 Generation Models” - Claude by Anthropic - 24 July 2026. ([Claude](https://claude.com/blog/the-new-rules-of-context-engineering-for-claude-5-generation-models?reference=FUZZ))

PASSAGE:
Anthropic recommends progressive disclosure and moving some guidance into selectively invoked skills rather than putting all instructions up front.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
Temporal thickness.

LOCAL MOVE:
Combines Ashby’s channel constraint with prompt thickness: context should be measured by timely recoverability, not static prompt volume.

SOURCE TERMS:
progressive disclosure; Skills; context; deferred tools; system prompt.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
A context can be thin at time t₀ yet operationally sufficient because it contains a route to thicker information at t₁.

QUESTION:
How late can a distinction arrive and still count as part of the operative description?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Is an agent’s ability to know where to retrieve information itself a compressed description of the missing information?

MECHANISM:
Meta-information routes the agent to additional task-specific distinctions only when the current state demands them.

FORMAL SHIFT:
ALL DESCRIPTION UP FRONT → JUST-IN-TIME REQUISITE DESCRIPTION.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Progressive context disclosure through Skills and selectively available resources.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
Thickness(t) = distinctions directly available + distinctions retrievable before the next irreversible decision.

TENSION:
Retrieval itself adds latency, uncertainty, tool failure, and attention costs.

MISSING:
A measure of optimal timing for context disclosure under bounded action horizons.

BOUNDARY:
This is a context-management architecture, not evidence that all tasks benefit from less up-front instruction.

CITATION TRAIL:
Claude 2026 → Ashby requisite access → thick prompting.

TEST:
Vary whether a Yellow Circle specification is fully explicit, stored in retrievable instructions, or revealed only after first action; compare correction cost and success.

PLATFORM:
Agent architecture / context engineering.

LINKS:
ZET-ASHBY-REQUISITE-ACCESS-1956; ZET-PROMPT-THICKNESS; ZET-DESCRIPTION-MIGRATION.

BIBTEX:
@misc{Shihipar2026ContextEngineering, author={Thariq Shihipar}, title={The New Rules of Context Engineering for Claude 5 Generation Models}, year={2026}, howpublished={Claude by Anthropic}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-INTERFACE-SCHEMA-AS-PROMPT-2026

TITLE:
Tool Design Can Carry Instructions That No Longer Need to Be Stated in the Prompt

SOURCE:
Thariq Shihipar - “The New Rules of Context Engineering for Claude 5 Generation Models” - Claude by Anthropic - 24 July 2026. ([Claude](https://claude.com/blog/the-new-rules-of-context-engineering-for-claude-5-generation-models?reference=FUZZ))

PASSAGE:
Anthropic contrasts supplying examples with designing interfaces whose schemas and allowed parameters guide model behavior.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
The tool interface as metapragmatic prompt.

LOCAL MOVE:
Connects GUI-as-language to agent tool use: action spaces themselves can teach the model which moves are grammatical.

SOURCE TERMS:
interfaces; tools; schemas; parameters; examples; context engineering.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
Instruction can disappear from prose because the affordance structure of the tool already constrains possible action.

QUESTION:
When is a schema merely an API contract, and when is it functioning as prompt?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can software architecture be analyzed as language addressed simultaneously to human and model implied readers?

MECHANISM:
Enumerated operations and typed parameters restrict and signal the space of plausible continuations.

FORMAL SHIFT:
PROMPT TELLS MODEL WHAT TO DO → TOOL ONTOLOGY MAKES CERTAIN DOINGS EXPRESSIBLE.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Tool/interface structure participates in context engineering.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
TOOL_SCHEMA = executable metapragmatics: it specifies what kinds of utterance/action can count as valid next moves.

TENSION:
A well-structured tool reduces ambiguity but may recreate Suchman’s problem of disciplining action through predefined categories.

MISSING:
Comparative evidence between schema-constrained and open-ended tool interfaces.

BOUNDARY:
Tool schemas do not determine correct use; model inference and surrounding context remain necessary.

CITATION TRAIL:
Claude context engineering → GUI language → Winograd/Suchman → thick prompting.

TEST:
Expose identical capability through a permissive generic action API and a semantically typed tool schema; keep user prompt fixed and compare error patterns.

PLATFORM:
Agent tools / HCI.

LINKS:
ZET-GUI-SPATIAL-LANGUAGE; ZET-METAPRAGMATICS; ZET-SUCHMAN-CATEGORIES-POLITICS-1994; ZET-IMPLIED-USER.

BIBTEX:
@misc{Shihipar2026ContextEngineering, author={Thariq Shihipar}, title={The New Rules of Context Engineering for Claude 5 Generation Models}, year={2026}, howpublished={Claude by Anthropic}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-SHNEIDERMAN-DIRECT-MANIPULATION-1983

TITLE:
Direct Manipulation Replaces Complex Command Syntax With Visible Objects and Incremental Action

SOURCE:
Ben Shneiderman - “Direct Manipulation: A Step Beyond Programming Languages” - IEEE Computer - 1983. ([Department of Computer Science](https://www.cs.umd.edu/~ben/papers/Shneiderman1983Direct.pdf))

PASSAGE:
Shneiderman centers visibility, rapid reversible incremental action, and replacement of complex command syntax by manipulation of the object of interest.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
Whether GUIs abolish language or relocate its distinctions.

LOCAL MOVE:
Provides the historical source against which “GUI as spatialized language” must argue rather than simply asserting it.

SOURCE TERMS:
direct manipulation; visible objects; rapid; reversible; incremental; command language syntax.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
The user experiences departure from programming language precisely because operational distinctions have migrated into persistent visible state and gesture.

QUESTION:
What remains language-like after textual command syntax has been displaced?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Is direct manipulation best understood as less language, or as grammar distributed into an environment?

MECHANISM:
Persistent representation of objects plus reversible actions replaces recall and symbolic command composition with perception-guided action.

FORMAL SHIFT:
COMMAND STRING → OBJECT + GESTURE + STATE + FEEDBACK.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Direct manipulation is explicitly framed as a step beyond programming languages.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
The command grammar survives as constraints on spatially situated valid actions even when command names disappear.

TENSION:
Calling all interaction “language” risks making the concept too broad to discriminate anything.

MISSING:
Criteria separating a spatial language-game from generic sensorimotor control.

BOUNDARY:
Shneiderman himself emphasizes replacement of command-language syntax; “spatialized language” is our reinterpretation.

CITATION TRAIL:
Shneiderman 1983 → Cognitive Dimensions interaction language → Put-That-There → GUI language.

TEST:
Represent one Yellow Circle operation as CLI, GUI, and multimodal deixis; inventory which distinctions move from recall into perceptual state at each transition.

PLATFORM:
HCI history.

LINKS:
ZET-GUI-SPATIAL-LANGUAGE; ZET-GREEN-BLACKWELL-INTERACTION-LANGUAGE-1998; ZET-PUT-THAT-THERE; ZET-ATTENTION-TAX.

BIBTEX:
@article{Shneiderman1983Direct, author={Ben Shneiderman}, title={Direct Manipulation: A Step Beyond Programming Languages}, journal={Computer}, year={1983}, volume={16}, number={8}, pages={57--69}, doi={10.1109/MC.1983.1654471}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-BROOKS-INTEGRITY-VS-HETEROGENEOUS-COORDINATION

TITLE:
Software Coordination May Need Conceptual Integrity Locally Without Requiring Semantic Uniformity Globally

SOURCE:
Frederick P. Brooks Jr. - The Mythical Man-Month - 1975. ([CMU School of Computer Science](https://www.cs.cmu.edu/afs/cs/academic/class/15712-s19/www/papers/mythicalmanmonth00fred.pdf))

PASSAGE:
Brooks argues that system design should reflect one coherent set of design ideas rather than many independent, uncoordinated ones.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
The level at which shared conceptual language is actually required in software.

LOCAL MOVE:
Pressures the earlier claim that software succeeds by making many minds “mutually compilable.” Brooks requires integrity in system design, but that does not yet establish that every participant must share one representation.

SOURCE TERMS:
conceptual integrity; system design; coordinated ideas; architecture; simplicity.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
A system may need a coherent public architecture while the people and disciplines coordinating through it retain different local models.

QUESTION:
Where must conceptual unity reside: in every participant, in the interface, in architecture, or only at translation boundaries?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can the Yellow Circle serve as a stable common object precisely because local representations remain heterogeneous?

MECHANISM:
A common architectural surface constrains externally visible behavior while allowing implementation and disciplinary descriptions to differ.

FORMAL SHIFT:
COORDINATION = SHARED MEANING → COORDINATION MAY REQUIRE ONLY SHARED INTERFACE INVARIANTS.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Brooks’s conceptual integrity concerns coherence of system design.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
Global artifact invariant can coexist with locally heterogeneous operational descriptions if translation boundaries preserve required distinctions.

TENSION:
Too much conceptual plurality can destroy usability and predictability; too much imposed unity can erase useful local distinctions.

MISSING:
Primary boundary-object collision and empirical evidence about coordination with non-consensual local representations.

BOUNDARY:
Brooks does not argue that every engineer must possess identical concepts.

CITATION TRAIL:
Brooks 1975 → software coordination → boundary-object hypothesis → Yellow Circle.

TEST:
Give three teams separate SVG, Logo, and GUI representations but one output acceptance test; compare coordination under shared vocabulary versus only shared artifact invariants.

PLATFORM:
Software architecture / CSCW.

LINKS:
ZET-SOFTWARE-COORDINATION; ZET-YELLOW-CIRCLE; ZET-LAW-REQUISITE-DESCRIPTION; ZET-TASK-OF-TRANSLATOR.

BIBTEX:
@book{Brooks1975Mythical, author={Frederick P. Brooks Jr.}, title={The Mythical Man-Month}, year={1975}, publisher={Addison-Wesley}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-ATTENTION-TAX-MUST-OUTRUN-COGNITIVE-DIMENSIONS

TITLE:
Attention Tax Is Redundant Unless It Explains Burden Migration Across a Coupled System

SOURCE:
Thomas R. G. Green and Alan F. Blackwell - Cognitive Dimensions of Information Artefacts: A Tutorial - 1998. ([Cambridge Computer Lab](https://www.cl.cam.ac.uk/~afb21/CognitiveDimensions/CDtutorial.pdf))

PASSAGE:
Cognitive Dimensions already analyzes whole notation-plus-environment systems through costs such as hidden dependencies, hard mental operations, diffuseness, viscosity, and premature commitment.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
The novelty test for attention tax.

LOCAL MOVE:
Opposes the graph’s own favored terminology. “Attention tax” should be retained only if it captures a mechanism not already covered by Cognitive Dimensions.

SOURCE TERMS:
cognitive dimensions; hard mental operations; visibility; hidden dependencies; diffuseness; premature commitment; notation; environment.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
Much of what we called language-specific attention cost already has a mature HCI vocabulary.

QUESTION:
What empirical prediction does attention tax make that a Cognitive Dimensions profile does not?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Is the new contribution not “cost” but the migration of requisite discriminatory work among human, model, interface, artifact, and environment?

MECHANISM:
Cognitive Dimensions characterizes usability tradeoffs in notational systems; the proposed attention-tax account adds participant-indexed allocation and transfer of requisite distinctions.

FORMAL SHIFT:
ATTENTION TAX = GENERIC DIFFICULTY → ATTENTION TAX = WHO MUST MAINTAIN WHICH ACTION-RELEVANT DISTINCTION, WHEN, AND AT WHAT TRANSFER BOUNDARY.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Cognitive Dimensions profiles notation-environment systems rather than supplying a single scalar cognitive-load measure.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
ATTENTION_LEDGER(d,t) = participant responsible for keeping distinction d available at decision time t.

TENSION:
If the ledger does not yield distinct predictions, “attention tax” should be discarded or subordinated to Cognitive Dimensions.

MISSING:
Head-to-head empirical comparison.

BOUNDARY:
Transformer attention mechanisms must not be treated as identical to human selective attention.

CITATION TRAIL:
Cognitive Dimensions → attention tax → Ashby requisite access → description migration.

TEST:
For one invariant Yellow Circle task, shift a coordinate distinction successively from human memory to GUI display to model inference; hold output constant and test whether the proposed ledger predicts performance changes beyond standard dimension ratings.

PLATFORM:
HCI theory / conceptual opposition.

LINKS:
ZET-ATTENTION-TAX; ZET-ASHBY-REQUISITE-ACCESS-1956; ZET-GREEN-BLACKWELL-INTERACTION-LANGUAGE-1998; ZET-YELLOW-CIRCLE.

BIBTEX:
@techreport{GreenBlackwell1998CognitiveDimensions, author={Thomas R. G. Green and Alan F. Blackwell}, title={Cognitive Dimensions of Information Artefacts: A Tutorial}, year={1998}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-DESCRIPTION-MIGRATION-NOT-DESCRIPTION-DISAPPEARANCE

TITLE:
Prompt Compression Is a Change of Address for Requisite Distinctions

SOURCE:
Collision: Ashby 1956; Green and Blackwell 1998; Claude context engineering 2026. ([Ashby Info](https://ashby.info/Ashby-Introduction-to-Cybernetics.pdf))

PASSAGE:
Ashby constrains regulation by communicable variety; Green and Blackwell permit information outside formal syntax; Anthropic distributes context beyond the user prompt.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
Description migration as a cross-historical mechanism.

LOCAL MOVE:
Turns a recurring metaphor into a discriminating research object: shorter explicit language is possible when required distinctions remain accessible through another channel.

SOURCE TERMS:
requisite variety; secondary notation; context engineering; system prompt; communication channel.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
The apparent disappearance of instruction can be empirically decomposed into transfers among representational locations.

QUESTION:
Can every successful prompt compression be traced to a specific destination for each removed requisite distinction?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Are there genuine cases where improved competence reduces the total number of distinctions required, rather than merely relocating them?

MECHANISM:
A distinction formerly maintained explicitly is supplied by learned regularity, environmental state, interface constraint, retrieval, or feedback.

FORMAL SHIFT:
LESS TEXT = LESS DESCRIPTION → LESS TEXT MAY = DESCRIPTION RELOCATED.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
No source uses “description migration” as defined here.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
For each deleted explicit distinction d:
LOCATE(d) ∈ {model competence, convention, tool schema, artifact, environment, history, feedback, genuinely unnecessary}.

TENSION:
Some compression may result from a better representation that truly reduces requisite distinctions rather than moving them.

MISSING:
A conservation law, if any; current evidence does not justify claiming strict conservation of descriptive complexity.

BOUNDARY:
Do not call description migration a proven conservation principle.

CITATION TRAIL:
Ashby → Cognitive Dimensions → Claude Code → thick prompting.

TEST:
Perform deletion experiments on Yellow Circle instructions and require an explicit causal account of where each successfully removed distinction is recovered.

PLATFORM:
Cross-domain theory / experimental method.

LINKS:
ZET-DESCRIPTION-MIGRATION; ZET-LAW-REQUISITE-DESCRIPTION; ZET-PROMPT-GOLF; ZET-CLAUDE-DESCRIPTION-MIGRATION-2026.

BIBTEX:
@misc{DescriptionMigrationCollision, title={Ashby 1956; Green and Blackwell 1998; Shihipar 2026}, year={2026}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-YELLOW-CIRCLE-DUAL-EXTREMES

TITLE:
The Yellow Circle Needs Two Opposed Benchmarks: Requisite Minimum and Requisite Explosion

SOURCE:
Collision: Ashby 1956; Bolt 1980; Claude context engineering 2026. ([Ashby Info](https://ashby.info/Ashby-Introduction-to-Cybernetics.pdf))

PASSAGE:
Bolt shows description compressed by deixis; Ashby constrains successful regulation; modern agent contexts show explicit prompt instructions can shrink while broader context remains operative.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
Prompt Golf and Software Chindōgu as complementary experimental instruments.

LOCAL MOVE:
Converts two jokes into opposed stress tests of the same theory.

SOURCE TERMS:
requisite variety; there; pointing; context; instruction; regulation.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
The same trivial final circle can probe both maximum compression and maximum unnecessary-but-operative mediation.

QUESTION:
What is the smallest explicit human intervention that still succeeds, and what is the largest functioning stack that can intervene without changing the accepted result?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Does operational complexity have any necessary relationship to perceptual complexity of output?

MECHANISM:
Prompt Golf removes explicit distinctions until failure; Chindōgu adds new representation boundaries and disturbances while preserving the target invariant.

FORMAL SHIFT:
ONE BENCHMARK → TWO CONJUGATE BENCHMARKS.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
NONE

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
MIN TEST: minimize human-explicit description subject to success.
MAX TEST: maximize operative mediation subject to same accepted artifact.

TENSION:
The Chindōgu arm can become arbitrary padding unless every added layer introduces a real transformation or failure opportunity.

MISSING:
A principled complexity metric covering languages, boundaries, state variables, feedback loops, and human actions.

BOUNDARY:
Maximum code length alone is not an adequate score.

CITATION TRAIL:
Yellow Circle → requisite description → Prompt Golf / Chindōgu.

TEST:
Implement matched endpoints: direct deictic minimum and maximal heterogeneous pipeline; compare pixel output, action-relevant disturbance variety, human attention, and recoverability.

PLATFORM:
Experimental benchmark design.

LINKS:
ZET-YELLOW-CIRCLE; ZET-PROMPT-GOLF; ZET-CHINDOGU-YELLOW-CIRCLE; ZET-LAW-REQUISITE-DESCRIPTION.

BIBTEX:
@misc{YellowCircleDualExtremes2026, title={Yellow Circle Dual-Extremes Experimental Formalization}, year={2026}}
```

```text
ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-YELLOW-CIRCLE-THREE-ORTHOGONAL-TESTS

TITLE:
Invariance, Aspect Instability, and Description Displacement Are Three Different Experiments

SOURCE:
Collision: Wittgenstein §§74, Bolt 1980, Ashby 1956. ([Wiley-Blackwell](https://www.blackwellpublishing.com/content/bpl_images/content_store/sample_chapter/9780631231271/Wittgenstein.pdf))

PASSAGE:
Wittgenstein links aspect to different action; Bolt replaces explicit description with situated gesture; Ashby constrains regulation by requisite variety.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
The experimental architecture of the Yellow Circle project.

LOCAL MOVE:
Separates three questions that had begun to blur into one another.

SOURCE TERMS:
aspect; order; pointing; there; requisite variety; regulation.

WHAT BECAME_STRANGE:
NONE

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
Holding output stable, holding stimulus stable, and holding task stable probe different mechanisms and should not share one score.

QUESTION:
Which experiment should be the primary benchmark and which should be supporting perturbations?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can these three tests triangulate the location, stability, and migration of operative distinctions?

MECHANISM:
Each test fixes a different variable and perturbs another.

FORMAL SHIFT:
YELLOW CIRCLE AS ONE DEMO → YELLOW CIRCLE AS THREE ORTHOGONAL EXPERIMENTS.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
NONE

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
INVARIANCE: same accepted object, different languages.
ASPECT: same stimulus, different language-game/action-space.
DISPLACEMENT: same task, remove explicit description and locate where requisite distinctions migrate.

TENSION:
The tests can interact; a changed representation may alter both aspect and distribution of description.

MISSING:
Experimental controls that isolate each axis.

BOUNDARY:
Do not infer causal mechanisms from cross-language comparison without controlled perturbations.

CITATION TRAIL:
Wittgenstein §74 + Bolt 1980 + Ashby 1956 → Yellow Circle experimental redesign.

TEST:
Pre-register fixed variables, perturbations, and success criteria separately for all three arms.

PLATFORM:
Experimental methodology.

LINKS:
ZET-YELLOW-CIRCLE; ZET-ASPECT-INSTABILITY; ZET-DESCRIPTION-MIGRATION; ZET-LAW-REQUISITE-DESCRIPTION.

BIBTEX:
@misc{YellowCircleThreeTests2026, title={Three Orthogonal Yellow Circle Tests}, year={2026}}
```

ZETTEL

ID:
ZET-ISER-IMPLIED-READER-AS-OPERATIVE-ROLE-1974

TITLE:
The Implied Reader Is a Role the Text Requires Someone to Perform

SOURCE:
Wolfgang Iser - The Implied Reader: Patterns of Communication in Prose Fiction from Bunyan to Beckett - 1974. ([Google Books](https://books.google.com/books/about/The_Implied_Reader.html?hl=id&id=Ix3uAAAAMAAJ&utm_source=chatgpt.com))

PASSAGE:
Iser develops literary effects through patterns of communication between text and reader; the work confronts readers with incomplete situations whose implications must be actualized in reading.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
The implied user of software as an operational role rather than an imagined demographic person.

LOCAL MOVE:
Replaces the loose claim that software is “written for an imagined audience” with a stronger distinction: an artifact can structurally presuppose a role that somebody must perform for it to work.

SOURCE TERMS:
reader; role; communication; expectations; perception; interpretation; literary text.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
The relevant analogue for software may not be “who the designer imagined” but “what operations the artifact requires its recipient to supply.”

QUESTION:
What user operations are structurally required for a GUI, API, prompt, or program to become executable?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can an interface possess an implied operator in the same sense that a text structures a reader role, even when no designer explicitly imagined that operator?

MECHANISM:
An artifact leaves relations incomplete and provides structures that solicit particular acts of completion from a recipient.

FORMAL SHIFT:
IMAGINED USER → STRUCTURALLY REQUIRED USER ROLE.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Iser analyzes patterns of textual communication and reader response rather than software interaction.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
IMPLIED_OPERATOR(interface) =
the minimal repertoire of distinctions and actions a participant must supply for valid continuation.

TENSION:
A software user changes machine state materially; Iser’s literary reader primarily actualizes a text in reading. The analogy is therefore not identity.

MISSING:
Primary passages from The Act of Reading specifying the “implied reader” as textual structure and structured act.

BOUNDARY:
Do not reduce the implied reader to a persona, demographic profile, or empirically observed user.

CITATION TRAIL:
Iser 1974 → implied reader → implied operator → attention tax → GUI language-game.

TEST:
Infer the implied operator independently from three Yellow Circle interfaces, then compare that inferred role with actual novice behavior.

PLATFORM:
Reader-response theory / HCI.

LINKS:
ZET-IMPLIED-USER; ZET-GUI-SPATIAL-LANGUAGE; ZET-ATTENTION-TAX; ZET-YELLOW-CIRCLE.

Woolgar treats the machine as text and examines usability trials as sites where relations between machine and user are actively configured rather than merely revealed.

MECHANISM:
Design and testing establish an interpretive boundary between the capacities built into the machine and those assigned to its prospective user.

Woolgar uses the metaphor of machine as text to analyze usability trials sociologically.

[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]

TENSION:
Configuration is not total determination; actual users can reinterpret, misuse, resist, and repurpose artifacts.

MISSING:

BOUNDARY:

PASSAGE:
Suchman challenges planning models of action that neglect the situated resources through which human activity is actually accomplished.

RESEARCH OBJECT:
The status of a prompt as plan, instruction, or situated resource.

LOCAL MOVE:
Opposes a strong operative-description model in which the prompt is treated as a precomputed program whose execution simply unfolds.

SOURCE TERMS:
plans; situated actions; circumstances; human action; communication; interaction; practical action.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
A prompt can successfully orient activity even when the eventual action sequence was neither stated nor inferable as a fixed plan beforehand.

QUESTION:
Is a prompt best modeled as a program, a constraint, a plan, a resource, or a move inside ongoing situated action?

DEEPER QUESTION:
What remains of “description becomes operation” if description does not determine the operation but only participates in its situated production?

MECHANISM:
Actors use partial representations together with evolving circumstances and feedback to produce locally appropriate action.

FORMAL SHIFT:
PROMPT → EXECUTION becomes PROMPT + SITUATION + EMERGENT ACTION ↔ FEEDBACK.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Suchman contrasts plan-centered models with situated accounts of purposeful action.

[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
NEXT_ACTION_t =
f(prompt, current_world_t, prior_actions, newly_available_resources).

TENSION:
Calling every prompt merely a resource would understate cases where formal descriptions tightly determine execution.

MISSING:
A taxonomy separating executable specification from orienting description in contemporary agents.

BOUNDARY:
Situated action does not imply absence of plans or goals.

CITATION TRAIL:
Suchman 1987 → thick prompting → world-to-world → critique/repair loop.

TEST:

HCI / situated action.

ZETTEL

PASSAGE:
Suchman’s copier study analyzes novice interaction with an “intelligent” machine whose design fails to accommodate resources routinely used in successful human communication.

Requisite context as asymmetrically available communicative resource.

LOCAL MOVE:
Qualifies thick prompting: information may exist in the situation yet remain useless if one participant lacks access to the resources through which humans normally recover its significance.

SOURCE TERMS:
communicative resources; mutual intelligibility; human-machine communication; inference; occasion; shared understanding.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
The problem may not be that the user supplied too little description; the machine may have been built to perceive too little of the situation.

QUESTION:
How much prompt engineering is compensation for impoverished machine access to the interactional world?

DEEPER QUESTION:
Does multimodal AI reduce the description burden primarily by making previously unavailable situated resources machine-readable?

MECHANISM:
Successful human interaction exploits context, sequence, gesture, repair, and practical circumstances that conventional interfaces often exclude from the machine’s perceptual field.

FORMAL SHIFT:
USER MUST EXPLICITATE MORE → SYSTEM CAN PERCEIVE MORE OF THE SITUATION.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Suchman analyzes asymmetries in communicative resources available to users and interactive machines.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
PROMPT_TAX increases when action-relevant environmental distinctions exist for the human but are outside the machine’s accessible channel.

TENSION:
Greater machine access to context can reduce explicit description while increasing privacy, surveillance, ambiguity, and interpretation risks.

MISSING:
A controlled comparison of text-only, vision-enabled, and gesture-enabled agents on identical deictic tasks.

BOUNDARY:
Machine perception of more context does not guarantee human-like situated understanding.

CITATION TRAIL:
Suchman 1987 → Put-That-There → requisite access → multimodal prompting.

TEST:

MECHANISM:
A symbolic pattern is interpreted against an existing situation in one direction and used to organize a future situation in the other.

Geertz’s models-of/models-for concern symbolic cultural patterns.

PASSAGE:
Geertz’s Rylean sequence distinguishes superficially similar eyelid movements by recovering the socially established structures under which they count as twitch, wink, imitation, rehearsal, or parody.

A non-quantitative definition of thickness.

LOCAL MOVE:
Kills the interpretation of “thick prompt” as merely long or information-dense. Geertzian thickness is discriminative structure, not descriptive volume.

thick description; twitch; wink; parody; code; interpretation; social action.

WHAT BECAME STRANGE:
A very long description can remain thin if it never supplies the distinction needed to tell which act is occurring.

QUESTION:

DEEPER QUESTION:
Can prompt thickness be operationalized as the number of rival action interpretations eliminated rather than amount of context supplied?

MECHANISM:
Contextual relations partition observationally similar behavior into different socially intelligible acts.

FORMAL SHIFT:
THICKNESS = INFORMATION QUANTITY → THICKNESS = ACTION-DISCRIMINATING CONTEXT.

SOURCE FORMALISM:
Geertz uses Ryle’s example to distinguish levels of interpretation in ethnographic description.

OUR FORMALIZATION:
[OUR FORMALIZATION - NOT SOURCE SYNTAX]
PROMPT_THICKNESS relative to action set A =
degree to which available context discriminates among rival next-actions in A.

TENSION:
Eliminating ambiguity is not always desirable; some language-games intentionally preserve several possible continuations.

MISSING:
A metric that handles useful ambiguity without equating thickness with determinacy.

BOUNDARY:
Geertz’s ethnographic thickness also concerns interpretive structures beyond immediate action selection.

CITATION TRAIL:
Ryle → Geertz 1973 → thick prompting → requisite description.

TEST:

Anthropology / prompt theory.

thick description; situated action; communicative resources; requisite variety; context.

A prompt can be incomplete in advance yet operationally sufficient if the missing distinctions become accessible before the relevant choice.

QUESTION:
